The AI is the reason the module exists. Everything before it is what the AI needs in order to know what it is
talking about.


---

---
title: "The registry the module reads"
order: 223
edit: false
---

`docs/help/registry.json` in each product repository is the single description of that product's
documentation set. This extension is a **reader** of it. The schema is owned by the generator in the product
repo (`scripts/docs-registry.mjs`) — if a field ever needs to change, it changes there first and this reader
follows.

## Shape

```json
{
  "version": 1,
  "areas": {
    "t3000": { "title": "t3000", "audience": "user", "noindex": false }
  },
  "pages": {
    "t3000/api-reference/data-points": {
      "title": "Data Points API",
      "area": "t3000",
      "chapter": "api-reference",
      "order": 20,
      "audience": "user",
      "ship": true,
      "owner": "docs",
      "tags": [],
      "keywords": [],
      "help_ids": [],
      "code": [],
      "reviewed": null
    }
  }
}
```

## Fields, and what they decide

| Field | Where | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `areas` | registry | the top level of the site: one entry per product area, with its default audience and whether it is excluded from the published site |
| `pages` | registry | every page, keyed by **page id** |
| `title` | page | the page's name, used for search, navigation and the tree |
| `area`, `chapter` | page | where the page sits; both must agree with the page id |
| `order` | page | position inside its chapter. Pages are ordered by it, then by title |
| `audience` | page | `user`, `developer` or `contributing`; falls back to the area's audience |
| `ship` | page | `false` keeps the page out of the shipped help build |
| `owner` | page | who is answerable for the page's accuracy |
| `tags`, `keywords`, `help_ids`, `code` | page | search facets, context-help ids, and the code globs a change should make the page stale. Empty is normal |
| `reviewed` | page | ISO date of the last human review, or `null`. `null` is a **count**, not a warning |

## Page id → file

The page id is the path under `docs/`, without the extension:

| Page id | File that must exist |
|---|---|
| `t3000/api-reference/data-points` | `docs/t3000/api-reference/data-points.md` |

That single rule is what makes a page "missing" checkable, and it is why this extension never guesses at
paths: the id is the path.

## Generated neighbours

The product repo also emits, and this extension may read:

- `docs/help/index.json` — per page: its headings, the last commit that touched it (author, sha, date), and the
  generator's `stale` verdict.
- `docs/help/page-meta.json` — the same provenance in the smaller shape the published page reads.

**Neither is required.** Absent, the extension works exactly as before; only provenance goes missing. A
malformed one is a **warning on that file** — the fix is to regenerate it in the product repo — never an error,
because it hides nothing else.

What the extension shows from them: a page's last change and headings in its tooltip, a `stale` marker on the
page (description and icon), and a stale count in the repository and area tooltips plus the status report. The
page's `code` globs are read from the same place, because that is what a `Draft update`'s commit range is read
against.

**`stale` is read, never computed here.** The verdict — a commit touched a path the page claims in `code`,
after its last review — belongs to the product repo's generator (`05-content-and-registry.md`, *Staleness*). Two answers
to that question would be worse than none, so the extension only repeats the generator's answer.

## Structural checks

Run on every refresh, cheap enough to run on 335 pages without thinking about it:

| Check | Severity | Reported on |
|---|---|---|
| the page has no title | error | the page |
| `area` is not declared in `areas{}` | error | the page |
| the id disagrees with `area`/`chapter` | error | the page |
| registered, but the file does not exist | error | `registry.json` |
| no `order` | warning | the page |
| two pages share one title | warning | both pages |
| two pages share one `order` inside a chapter | warning | both pages |
| the registry declares no pages | warning | `registry.json` |
| the registry is not valid JSON | error | `registry.json` |

**Deliberately not checked here:** prose quality, links, images and staleness. Those belong to the product
repo's own gates (`npm run docs:verify`, the media audit, `docs-owed`) and to the AI phase, where a commit
range can be read. Duplicating them in the extension would create two answers to one question.

## The feedback file — a proposal, not a contract

Where a feedback row comes from is not settled: the feedback design
(`09-feedback-subscriptions-notifications.md`) assumes an endpoint on the T3000 server, while the management
overview (`16-overview.md`) describes a different hosting model. This module reads the smallest shape
that carries what routing needs, per repository:

```json
{
  "version": 1,
  "rows": [
    { "page": "t3000/api-reference/data-points", "kind": "wrong", "note": "…", "at": "2026-09-01", "state": "open", "by": "…" }
  ]
}
```

`page` is a page id, `at` is an ISO date, and `state` is `open` or anything else for handled. A row whose page
is not in the registry is still reported — with its id, as plain text rather than a link — because that is a
finding about the row, not a reason to drop it. With no such file, **Feedback by Owner** says the source is not
agreed rather than reporting no feedback.

**This shape is the extension's proposal**, and the docs owner accepts or replaces it. It is the only part of the feedback design that is not settled.


---

---
title: "temco-editor"
order: 230
edit: false
---

The editor for a Markdown page: one module, one editor, three layers. It owns the surface and nothing about what a page
*is* — that stays in `temco-docs`.

## Identifiers

| | |
|---|---|
| Extension id | `temcocontrols.temco-editor` |
| View type | `temcoEditor.markdown` — **Temco Editor**, `priority: "default"` for `*.md` |
| Commands | `temcoEditor.open` · `temcoEditor.openSource` · `temcoEditor.preview` · `temcoEditor.diff` · `temcoEditor.pageProperties` |
| Namespace | `temcoEditor` |
| Storage | none |
| Depends on | `temcocontrols.temco-docs` (its model, through `src/docs.ts`) |

## Why it exists

Writing a page in a text editor means thinking about pipes and asterisks; a WYSIWYG surface means writing the page. The
editor used to live inside `temco-docs` as `temcoDocs.page`, next to the registry, the tree, the builds and the release
report — 494 KB of documentation domain beside ~250 KB of editor. This module is that 250 KB, given its own front door.

It also replaced its own prototype: for a while there were two editors to compare. The comparison is over, the winning
affordances are here — a **selection menu** for the marks, a **drag handle** beside a block, a **placeholder** on an empty
page — and the prototype is deleted.

## The three layers

```text
the surface      main.tsx · ribbon.tsx · reading.tsx      asks for named operations, reads a snapshot of the caret
   ↕ contract    webview/markdown/contract.ts             PageState · PageActions · Mark · ListKind — types only
the engine       webview/markdown/engine/tiptap/          the only place a Tiptap package is named
```

The rule is enforced by reading, not by trust: `grep -r "@tiptap" webview/markdown/*.tsx webview/shared/` returns
nothing, `scripts/check-shortcuts.mts` reads the shortcuts out of `engine/tiptap/document.tsx` rather than assuming them,
and replacing Tiptap means deleting one folder.

The host is the same shape from the other side: `src/` knows a document, a panel, an edit and a line ending, and no
`Editor`, `Node`, `Mark`, `PluginKey` or `Transaction`. What a page *is* — its media folder, the version it describes, the
pages a link may reach — it asks `temco-docs` for, through `src/docs.ts`.

## What it owns

- the view type, the provider, and the commands that reach it;
- the bridge to the file: one `WorkspaceEdit` per save, the file's own line endings, and the ordered-write guard that
  keeps a caret still while two writes land out of order;
- the Markdown contract (`src/markdown.ts`) — the rules that make an unedited page open and save to the same bytes;
- the surface: the ribbon, the selection menu, the block handle, find, the outline rail, read mode, zoom, capture;
- its bundle and the one bundler step that produces it.

## What it must not own

- **the registry, the pages model, the builds, the release report** — `temco-docs`;
- **where an image lands.** `media/`, the file name and the attachment record are the model's; this module supplies the
  bytes and the words and asks (`landImage`);
- **a second Markdown dialect.** The round trip is one implementation. If Tiptap's own serialiser would be better, that
  is a change to the contract, proved by `check-page` first.

## What was learned the hard way

- **A task list was destroyed twice** — on open, because `marked` writes `<ul><li><input type="checkbox">` and Tiptap
  wants `ul[data-type="taskList"]`; on save, because `turndown-plugin-gfm` wants the `<input>` as the item's first child
  and Tiptap renders it inside a `<label>`. Both shapes are now accepted on the way in (`engine/tiptap/taskLists.mts`)
  and the checkbox is read wherever it sits on the way out (`src/markdown.ts`).
- **Tiptap merges attribute parsers over a rule's `getAttrs`**, so `checked` had to be parsed in `addAttributes`, not in
  the parse rule.
- **A missing bundle is a blank tab, not an error**: `bridge.ts` asks for `markdown.js` and `markdown.css` by name, a 404
  on a script fires no `onerror`, and `compile-extension:<name>` cleans `out/` before `tsc` — which deletes the bundle.
  `check-surface.mts` loads the built file, so a stale or missing bundle fails the gate instead of the tab.

## Gates

`npm run vscode:prepublish`, in order: `check-commands` · `check-fluent` · `check-types` (host and webview, separately) ·
`compile` · `check-page` (13 round-trip cases) · `check-task-lists` (a real editor, both shapes) · `check-outline`
(29 cases, no browser) · `check-shortcuts` (every key the bar teaches is bound) · `build-webview` · `check-surface` (the
built bundle in jsdom: it starts, draws the page, writes one edit back, keeps its checkboxes, and survives a round trip
through Read mode).

From the repository root, `npm run compile-temco` builds the extensions **and** both webview bundles — the only correct
way to rebuild after a `tsc` run, which cleans `out/` first.


---

---
title: "`temco-projects`"
order: 240
edit: false
---

A documentation project as a first-class thing: its name, its sources, its outputs, its builds, and the pages it produces. One page — the edit form — and one command that opens the project's pages.

| | |
|---|---|
| Folder | `extensions/temco-projects` |
| Extension id | `temcocontrols.temco-projects` |
| Namespace | `temcoProjects` |
| State | active |
| Pages | `temcoProjects.project` (the form) — opening a project shows its pages through `temcoDocs.documents` |
| Settings | none |
| Requires | `temco-store` (memory), `temco-ui` (page columns), `temco-repositories` (sources and their scripts) |
| Required by | `temco-docs` — for the one signal that brings the Library's project rows back |

## Responsibilities, one file each

| Module | Owns |
|---|---|
| `src/extension.ts` | activation, the output channel a build writes to, and the change signal |
| `src/projectHub.ts` | ~~one project in depth~~ — removed: it read the same counts the Library and the dashboard already show, and clicking a project now selects it and opens its pages |
| `src/projectPage.ts` | the form: name, version, audience, owner, sources, outputs |
| `src/project.ts` | the commands that act on a project, and the argument tolerance they need to be called from a row |
| `src/removal.ts` | removing a project's folder with it: the question that names the folder, the delete, and what happens to the sources that recorded a path inside it |
| `src/projectBuild.ts` | building a project's outputs: the generator first when the repository has one, then one script per ticked output, judged by the files they left behind |
| `src/surface.ts` | the two view types |
| `src/store.ts` · `src/ui.ts` · `src/repositories.ts` · `src/aiModule.ts` | what this module reads of the store, the UI rules, the repository scripts and the AI — each in one file, because an extension cannot import another extension's sources |

## Commands

`temcoProjects.newProject` · `openProject` · `editProject` · `removeProject` · `selectProject` · `clearProject` · `addSource` · `buildProject` — eight, each doing one thing to a project.

## Boundary rules

- **A project is a name and a set of relationships**, not a copy of anything. Its sources stay in their repositories, its pages stay where they are written, and the store holds the rows that tie them together.
- **The pages a project has come from the store.** A read recorded them; this module does not parse a registry, which is why it needs nothing from `temco-docs`.
- **A build is invoked, never reimplemented.** The script is the product repository's own; it runs in that repository's folder, its exit code is reported and never trusted, and the verdict is the file that did not exist before and does now. Every ticked output that has a script runs, once each — asking for a site and a PDF runs both. The deploying form of the help build is never invoked.
- **Removing a project takes its folder with it.** The row and everything recorded against it go, and so does the folder the project owns — `<projectsFolder>/<slug>/` (`temcoRepositories.projectsFolder`, or the working area when it is empty) — holding its `docs/`, its build and what that build produced. The confirmation names that folder before it is deleted. No origin is ever written to and nothing is ever pushed; a clone in the shared `source/` folder is untouched.
- **The AI is optional.** This module declares `temco-store`, `temco-ui` and `temco-repositories`, never `temco-ai`: its pages ask the AI for facts when that module is installed and say so when it is not.
- **The scripts vocabulary is the repositories module's.** Which of the repository's own scripts exist, and what each is for, is a fact about a repository — this module reads the list (`src/repositories.ts`) rather than keeping a second copy.


---

---
title: "`temco-repositories`"
order: 250
edit: false
---

The organisation and the local copies of it: the repository list, the working area, and reading a source into the platform's memory.

| | |
|---|---|
| Folder | `extensions/temco-repositories` |
| Extension id | `temcocontrols.temco-repositories` |
| Namespace | `temcoRepositories` |
| State | active |
| Page | **The Repositories** (`temcoRepositories.page`) — a webview tab, opened by its own command |
| Settings | `temcoRepositories.organisation`, `temcoRepositories.workspaceFolder`, `temcoRepositories.projectsFolder` |
| Requires | `temco-store` (the platform's memory), `temco-ui` (where a page opens) |
| Required by | `temco-docs` — for the code files of a source and the material a new page is drafted from |

## Responsibilities, one file each

| Module | Owns |
|---|---|
| `src/extension.ts` | activation, the page command, the output channel, the change signal, and the API |
| `src/repository.ts` | the organisation's repositories as an API read (paged, with a token when there is one), and a source's code files — from a working tree or from `git/trees` |
| `src/read.ts` | reading a source: the registry it declares, parsed for pages, recorded in the catalog with what the read concluded; the token in secret storage; opening a file over the API |
| `src/workarea.ts` | the working area: where a clone goes, whether one is there, how big it is, and clone / pull / remove |
| `src/repositoriesPage.ts` | the page: what the organisation has, what has been read, what has a local copy, and the buttons for each |
| `src/surface.ts` | the page's view type (`temcoRepositories.page`) |
| `src/store.ts` · `src/ui.ts` | what this module reads of `temco-store` and `temco-ui` — the copies of their shapes |

## Commands

`temcoRepositories.page` · `readSource` · `readAllSources` · `removeSource` · `revealSource` · `setSourceBranch` · `openFile` · `setToken` · `fetchSource` · `pullSource` · `openClone` · `removeClone` · `changeWorkspaceFolder` · `openWorkFolder` — the working area and reading, each with its own command rather than a mode.

Two of those are for other modules rather than for the palette, and are deliberately not declared: `projectWorkFolder` answers where a project's folder is and whether it exists, and `deleteProjectFolder` deletes it. `temco-projects` asks both when a project is removed, so the confirmation can name the folder before it goes — the working area is this module's, and the project row is that one's.

## Boundary rules

- **Nothing is written to a repository, and nothing is ever pushed.** Every write this module makes is to the catalog; git is used for `clone` and `pull --ff-only` and for reading history, and never for a change to a tracked file.
- **The working area is outside every repository** (`temcoRepositories.workspaceFolder`, default `~/temco`), so a clone can never be mistaken for a source. A project's own folder is placed by a second setting — `temcoRepositories.projectsFolder`, empty meaning "beside the clones" — so projects can be kept apart from the shared `source/` without moving it.
- **A token lives in secret storage**, keyed `temcoRepositories.githubToken`. It is never written to a setting, a file or the catalog.
- **Reading is explicit.** No read, clone or pull happens on its own: the first read offers a local copy, and every act after that is a command a person ran.
- **What a registry means is not decided here.** This module reads the file and records the rows; the registry's shape, its checks and its areas are `temco-docs`', which is why this module needs nothing from it.
- **The page opens where our pages open.** That rule is `temco-ui`'s — one group, never a split — and it is read here rather than re-decided (`src/ui.ts`).


---

---
title: "`temco-store`"
order: 260
edit: false
---

The platform's own memory: one SQLite file per window, the selection, and the naming rules the tables are keyed by. It is the base every other module stands on.

| | |
|---|---|
| Folder | `extensions/temco-store` |
| Extension id | `temcocontrols.temco-store` |
| Namespace | `temcoStore` |
| State | active |
| Surface | one command (`temcoStore.status`) — no view, no container, no settings |
| Requires | nothing |
| Required by | `temco-docs` (and, through it, `temco-ai`); `temco-repositories` and `temco-projects` when they are split out |

## Responsibilities, one file each

| Module | Owns |
|---|---|
| `src/extension.ts` | activation, and the API below |
| `src/api.ts` | the published contract: `available()`, `catalog()`, `CatalogUnavailable`, `selection`, `slugOf` |
| `src/catalog/schema.ts` | the tables, one per idea, and `SCHEMA_VERSION` — a bump is a migration, and a newer database is refused in words |
| `src/catalog/store.ts` | `node:sqlite`: the open, the pragmas, the schema, and every statement the platform runs — sources, projects (`documents` in the tables), their sources, recipes, builds, artifacts, commits, pages, scans, events |
| `src/selection.ts` | which project the window is on and the page it last opened from it, in `workspaceState`, with the one signal the views and the AI listen to |
| `src/names.ts` | `slugOf` — the name → key rule the tables assume |
| `src/commands.ts` | the one command: what the store is, where it is, what it holds |

## The catalog, in one paragraph

A **source** is somewhere documentation was read from: a repository, a folder, an archive, with the branch and the local copy when there is one. A **project** (the table is still `documents`; renaming it buys a migration and nothing else) is a name, a version, an audience, an owner, the sources it is made of, and the outputs it produces. A **read** records what a scan found — the page ids, their paths, their owners — and what it concluded, so a list can say "read, 42 pages" without re-reading anything. **Builds** and **artifacts** are what the project's own pipeline produced, keyed by path so a second build of the same site does not turn one output into two rows. **Events** are what happened, and `unseenEvents()` is how a surface says "something moved". **Commits** are the history a draft is about, per source and per page.

## Boundary rules

- **It has no domain opinions.** It stores and returns rows; it does not decide what a project is, what to read, or what to build. Those are `temco-projects`' and `temco-repositories`' questions.
- **It knows no view.** No webview, no tree, no icon: the command prints to an output channel and that is all.
- **It is not `temco-sql`.** That module is about the databases a *product* uses (registers, queries, export). This is the platform's own file, and the reserved module keeps its meaning.
- **It is never a repository's storage.** Nothing is written into a product repository: the database lives in this module's `globalStorageUri` (`<storage>/catalog/catalog.db`) and the materialised trees in a `sources/` cache beside it.
- **A caller passes no context.** `Catalog.open(context)` exists for compatibility at the call sites, and the argument is ignored — the file is the store's, opened with the store's own storage.

## Callers

A consumer mirrors what it needs in its own `src/store.ts` (an extension cannot import another extension's sources) and declares this module in `extensionDependencies`, so the exports exist before its own `activate()` runs. `extensions/temco-docs/src/store.ts` is the copy to follow: it re-presents the same names — `Catalog`, `CatalogUnavailable`, `Selection`, `slugOf` — so a call site reads as it did before the split.


---

---
title: "`temco-tools`"
order: 270
edit: false
---

The device and data surfaces, in the `Tools` activity-bar container.

| | |
|---|---|
| Folder | `extensions/temco-tools` |
| Extension id | `temcocontrols.temco-tools` |
| Namespace | `temcoTools` |
| State | active |
| Views | **Devices**, **I/O Studio**, **BACnet Studio**, **Network**, **SQL Studio** — one view per studio, one row per surface |
| Pages | one per studio (`temcoTools.devices`, `temcoTools.io`, `temcoTools.bacnet`, `temcoTools.network`, `temcoTools.sql`), each a stack of folded surfaces |
| Requires | `temco-ui` |

## Why one module

The platform documents a `temcoTools` container "declared by the first tools module", and four separate modules
(`temco-device-manager`, `temco-bacnet`, `temco-nettools`, `temco-sql`) are reserved for the day each has a backend.
Until then this module is their container: the surfaces exist, are navigable, and are drawn with the same page kit as
the rest of the platform, so the design is decided now and the backends fill it rather than redesign it.

## What is real, and what is sample

| Real | Sample |
|---|---|
| the container, the five views, the side-bar rows, the pages and their sections | the rows: `src/data.ts` is the mock's sample — devices, points, objects, hosts, tables, queries |
| the navigation: a row opens its section on the studio's page; a head button opens another section of the same page | the numbers and states |
| the vocabulary: `temco-ui`'s `page.css` — page head, group rules, tables, badges | — |

The sample lives in exactly one file. When a backend arrives (a socket, a stack, a driver), it replaces `data.ts` and
no page has to change.

## Files

| File | Owns |
|---|---|
| `src/extension.ts` | the five views, the `temcoTools.open` command, and the studio page that reveals or re-renders |
| `src/pages.ts` | the surfaces of each studio: tables, sections, and the page that stacks them |
| `src/data.ts` | the sample behind every row, in one place |
| `src/ui.ts` | what it reads of `temco-ui`: the page shell and the click-to-command script |

## Boundary rules

- **A page belongs to a studio, not to a surface.** The side bar names a surface, the page opens it; five pages per
  studio would be five places to keep the same head in step.
- **Nothing is reported as live.** A page that showed a sample value without saying so would be worse than an empty
  page; the module README and the code say it, and the day a backend lands the sample file is deleted.
- **No writes.** Nothing here sends a command to a controller, a stack or a database.


---

---
title: "`temco-ui`"
order: 280
edit: false
---

The application's shared UI conventions, and nothing else.

| | |
|---|---|
| Folder | `extensions/temco-ui` |
| Extension id | `temcocontrols.temco-ui` |
| Namespace | `temcoUi` |
| State | active |
| Surface | none — no command, no view, no setting |
| Requires | nothing |
| Required by | every module that opens a page (`temco-repositories`, `temco-docs`, …) |

## Responsibilities, one file each

| Module | Owns |
|---|---|
| `src/extension.ts` | activation, and the API below |
| `src/columns.ts` | `pageColumn()` and `fileColumn()` — the one tab group our pages open in, and the same group for the files they open beside them |
| `src/rows.ts` | `rowSlug()` — what a command argument names: a slug, a row, or a row's own command argument |
| `src/page.ts` | `page()` — the document around a page, `escapeHtml()`, and the click-to-command script a page runs |
| `media/page.css` | the page vocabulary: what a module draws inside a page tab |

## The page vocabulary

The mock site (`docs/mock`) is where this application's pages were designed, and `media/page.css` is that design
shipped to the product: the same class names and shapes as `docs/mock/css/views.css`, so a page can be designed in
the mock and built here without being re-designed.

What changes between the two hosts is only colour. The mock owns literal colours; the product reads the theme the
person chose. The top block of `page.css` is that whole difference — every mock colour role (`--fg`, `--border`,
`--accent`, `--ok`…) is mapped onto a `--vscode-*` variable, and every rule after it means the same thing in both
places. Sizes follow `--vscode-font-size`, so raising the editor font size makes a page larger rather than ignored.

`page(body, { title, script })` returns a complete document: the stylesheet inlined (a webview that links a file
needs `localResourceRoots` and a URI that survives the server case; a page needs neither), a content-security policy,
and the body inside `.view > .view__inner`, which is the width rule the mock already uses.

**A page runs a command by message, not by link.** Anything carrying `data-command` posts
`{ type: 'command', command, args }` back to its module when clicked; `commandScript` is that script and
`runCommand(message)` is the half that runs it. A page therefore never decides what a command means, and a module can
audit the whole of what its pages can do in one place. It also means `enableScripts` buys exactly one behaviour
instead of a small application per page.

## The column rule, and why it is a rule

**Our pages are tabs in one group.** The dashboard, a project, the AI page, the repositories page and the documentation pages they open share a single tab strip, so whichever one is in front fills the editor area. Never a split: a page in half the window is not "shown", and two groups means one of them is always too narrow.

The rule was settled on 2026-10-14 after two wrong answers, both of which asked "is the tab in front one of ours?" and fell back to `Beside` — which is what produced the split, because a webview tab does not report its view type the way that check assumed. It asks nothing about what is in front now: it finds the group that already holds a page of ours and opens there, so our pages can only ever occupy one group, whatever is clicked, from wherever.

The prefix it matches is `temco`, so every module's view type counts as one of ours — `temcoDocs.*`, `temcoAi.page`, `temcoRepositories.page`.

## Why a module and not a helper inside one of the others

The rule lived in `temco-docs/src/surface.ts`, which was right while every page was that module's. Once pages spread across modules, the rule had to belong to something *none* of them owns: a page opened from `temco-ai` and a page opened from `temco-repositories` must land in the same strip, and a copy of the rule per module is a copy that will diverge — which is exactly how the split it exists to prevent came back the first two times.

## Consumers

Declare it in `extensionDependencies` and read it through a bridge of your own — `extensions/temco-repositories/src/ui.ts` is the copy to follow — because an extension cannot import another extension's sources.


---

---
title: "`temco-workbench`"
order: 290
edit: false
---

The application's own surfaces. It is the only module that is not a domain: it owns the **Library** (the index of what every module has), the **dashboard**, the **needs page**, and the three layout decisions that come with them — which side panel and which tab open at startup, and which sidebar section comes forward when the project changes.

| | |
|---|---|
| Folder | `extensions/temco-workbench` |
| Extension id | `temcocontrols.temco-workbench` |
| Namespace | `temcoWorkbench` |
| State | active |
| View | **Library** (`temcoWorkbench.library`), contributed into the `temco` activity-bar container that the documentation module declares |
| Pages | `temcoWorkbench.overview` (the dashboard), `temcoWorkbench.activity` (what the platform recorded), `temcoWorkbench.openNeeds` (what wants a person), `temcoWorkbench.welcome` (the page a new window opens on) |
| Setting | `temcoWorkbench.start.open` — `welcome` · `project` · `page` · `home` · `none` (default `welcome`) · `temcoWorkbench.start.panel` — the left panel's item revealed at startup (default `temcoDashboard`) · `temcoWorkbench.updateChannel` and the `branding` · `ai` · `publishing` keys the Settings pages write |
| Requires | `temco-store`, `temco-ui`, `temco-repositories`, `temco-docs` |
| Required by | `temco-docs` — nothing; the dependency runs one way, and the workbench reads the documentation module's model |

## Responsibilities, one file each

| Module | Owns |
|---|---|
| `src/extension.ts` | activation: builds the three surfaces, wires their signals, and decides the first tab |
| `src/sidebar.ts` | the side-bar groups that are not a data view — Platform, Needs Work, Site, Delivery, Profile, Learn, Support — registered here because a container cannot declare its order in the activity bar |
| `src/dashboard.ts` | the dashboard's two pages: **Overview** (what the platform holds, what is waiting, the pipeline, the activity) and **Recent Activity** |
| `src/helpPages.ts` | Welcome, Help, Keyboard Shortcuts, Logs, Report an Issue, About — and the one preference Welcome carries |
| `src/settingsPages.ts` | the five settings pages (General, Modules, Branding, AI Runtime, Publishing). Every row is a declared setting, so a write lands in `settings.json` and the window obeys it at once; the page reads the configuration back and never keeps a value of its own |
| `src/needsPage.ts` | what wants a person, gathered from every module into one list with the reason and where the fix is |
| `src/learnPages.ts` | Temco Learn as a reader sees it: Home, T3000, an article, Downloads, Learning Paths |
| `src/surface.ts` | the page view types |
| `src/docs.ts` · `src/store.ts` · `src/ui.ts` · `src/repositories.ts` · `src/aiModule.ts` | what it reads of the documentation module, the store, the UI rules, the repository scripts and the AI — each in one file, because an extension cannot import another extension's sources |

## Commands

`temcoWorkbench.home` (the dashboard) · `temcoWorkbench.openNeeds` (the needs page) · `temcoWorkbench.welcome` (the welcome page) · `temcoWorkbench.toggleWelcomeStartup` (the checkbox in its footer, which writes `temcoWorkbench.start.open`) · `temcoWorkbench.settings` (the settings page) · `temcoWorkbench.setStartOpen` (the one command a row on it runs) · `temcoWorkbench.help` (the help page). Everything the Library's rows and menus do is another module's command — the menus are here, the work is not.

## What the pages refuse to invent

The mock has surfaces whose content this product cannot honestly produce, and none of them were copied across:

| Mock page | Here |
|---|---|
| Help › Keyboard Shortcuts, Help › Logs | Not built. VS Code publishes neither list to an extension, and a page that guessed would be a page that lies. The Help page carries what is real instead: the build, the module register, the platform string, and the issue tracker. |
| Workspaces | Removed. A workspace was the mock's folder-above-the-project level, drawn over a fixed sample (`T3000`, `T3-BACnet`, …) with counts nothing recorded, and a “create” that wrote only into the window. The product's unit is the **project**, which the catalog holds; its sources are the folders, and New Project is the wizard the sample stood in for. |
| Devices, I/O Studio, BACnet, Network, SQL Studio | Not built. Those modules are reserved — a page over a module that does not exist would be a mock inside the product. |

## One home: Welcome, the VS Code way

A window that opens on a page you did not ask for has to earn it, so the welcome page carries only what a person can act on: six ways in (each a button running another module's command), three walkthroughs that list their steps instead of claiming progress, and a footer whose single checkbox writes the setting that decides the first tab. There is no picture of the product on it.

The arrangement is VS Code's, deliberately:

- **Welcome is the first tab.** `start.open` defaults to `welcome`, so a launch answers "what is this and where do I begin" before it shows any work.
- **The page owns the switch.** Its footer checkbox *Show the welcome page at startup* writes `temcoWorkbench.start.open` — there is no second place to turn it off, and the checkbox and the setting cannot disagree.
- **Help ▸ Welcome brings it back**, as does the palette, every time.
- **The Dashboard is not a second home.** It is the activity-bar container you go to *after* Welcome — Overview (what the platform holds and what is waiting), Recent Activity, Needs Work. VS Code's equivalent is Explorer: state you click into, never a page that opens itself beside the welcome page.

The two only ever fought because either could be the first tab. Now one is the default and the other is a click, and each has one question: Welcome asks *where do I start*, the Overview asks *what is in it and what needs me*. With the checkbox off the chain never ends at the welcome page — `project` → the page you were last on → the overview — so turning it off means what it says; an empty window gets the overview, which has its own empty state and a *New Project* button. **Coverage & Health** is gone as a third dashboard page: the documentation module draws that report from the same registry with the same figures, so the Overview's Knowledge card links to it instead of duplicating it.

## Where a settings page writes

Every row on the five Settings pages is a declared setting, so everything a page decides is in the profile's `settings.json`:

| Page | Keys |
|---|---|
| General | `workbench.colorTheme` · `temcoWorkbench.start.panel` · `workbench.breadcrumbs.enabled` · `telemetry.telemetryLevel` · `temcoWorkbench.updateChannel` |
| Modules | none — the rows describe what is loaded, and every button runs another module's command |
| Branding | `temcoWorkbench.branding.productName` · `.accent` — the name the pages draw, and the accent written into `workbench.colorCustomizations` |
| AI Runtime | `temcoAi.model` · `.runtime` · `.modelFolder` · `.allowUnpinned` · `.idleMinutes` · `.serverPort` · `.serverIdleMinutes` · `.platformApi` — the AI module's own keys, so the page writes exactly what that module reads |
| Publishing | `temcoWorkbench.publishing.site` · `.path` · `.channel` · `.website` · `.pdf` · `.offlineHelp` · `.searchIndex` · `.aiIndex` |

Two facts are not settings and are not written there: **when a row last changed** and **what Test Connection last read back**. Both are kept in the profile's own state rather than in the file, because they record what happened rather than what a person chose. `Restore Defaults` removes the value instead of writing a copy of today's default, so the manifest stays the only place a default is stated.

## Boundary rules

- **The Library is an index, not a domain.** It shows what the store recorded and what the documentation module published; it owns no data of its own and keeps no copy.
- **A row's action belongs to the module that owns the thing.** *Read a source* is `temcoRepositories.readSource`, *Build the project* is `temcoProjects.buildProject`, a page's *Rename* is `temcoDocs.renamePage`. The Library names them; it never re-implements one.
- **Nothing here writes.** The only state it changes is the project being worked on, and that is the store's own command.
- **The AI is optional for it too.** The Library's AI row appears only when that module is installed — asked, never assumed.
- **The Workspace is not its view.** The tree of pages is the documentation module's own view; the workbench only *brings it forward* by id when the project changes.


---

---
title: "The application flow"
order: 400
edit: false
---

> **Superseded for the Docs center.** The window layout, the Pages tree, the journey and the Publish flow are defined in [redesign.md](redesign). Where this page disagrees with it, `redesign.md` wins.

The information architecture as built: the left toolbar, the four panels, the page in the middle, and the one right-hand panel. This is the confirmed design — `authoring-flow.md` is the capture stage inside it.

## Left toolbar

```
 ┌─────┐
 │  ⧉  │  Explorer          VS Code's own
 │  ⌕  │  Search            VS Code's own
 │  ▦  │  Dashboard         projects · overview · needs you · recent activity
 │  ▤  │  Documentation     pages · project · pipeline · working set
 │  ⧉  │  Repositories      org → repository → code files
 │  ✦  │  AI                models · drafts waiting
 │  ◫  │  Learn             site · delivery
 │  ▥  │  SQL Studio        query & browse · schema & maintenance
 │  ⚙  │  Settings          profile · platform
 │  ?  │  Help              learn · support
 └─────┘
```

The shell's `ORDER` holds the eight module containers (`temcoDashboard · temco · temcoRepos · temcoAiHub · temcoLearn · temcoSql · temcoSettings · temcoHelp`); Explorer and Search are VS Code's and come first.

## Dashboard — four views

| View | What the panel shows | The page behind it |
|---|---|---|
| **Projects** (`temcoProjects.projects`) | a row per project: sources · built · current, with `✎` and `ⓘ` | clicking a row opens the **project page** (`temcoDocs.projectPage`): counts, sources, outputs, build buttons |
| **Overview** (`temcoWorkbench.platformView`) | **the facts as rows** — Pages · Reviewed · Stale · Issues · Sources · Outputs · Build | each row opens its page (the tree, the report, Problems, the project form, Build Center) |
| **Needs You** (`temcoDocs.queue`) | the queue's groups; a group opens its page | the **queue page** (`temcoDocs.needsPage`): screenshots to place, retake these, never reviewed — each with its act |
| **Recent activity** (`temcoWorkbench.activityView`) | the activity row | the **activity page** (`temcoWorkbench.activity`): every recorded event, newest first |

```
 DASHBOARD                                        ⟳
 ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ PROJECTS                                              ＋        │
 │   T3000 Documentation          3 sources · built 12:04 · current│
 │                                                [ ✎ ] [ ⓘ ]    │
 │   My First Docs                1 source · never built          │
 │   Legacy Import                1 source · built 09:12          │
 ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ OVERVIEW                                                       │
 │   Pages      318       Reviewed  304                           │
 │   Stale       14       Issues    0 · 3                          │
 │   Sources      3       Outputs   Site · help                    │
 │   Build      Build Center                                       │
 ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ NEEDS YOU                                               (7)    │
 │   ▸ Screenshots to place (1) · Retake these (4) · Never… (2)    │
 ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ RECENT ACTIVITY                                                │
 │   Recent Activity                        20 events              │
 └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

`✎` = *Edit the Pages* (`temcoProjects.openProject`) → the project becomes the one being worked on and the Documentation panel shows its tree.
`ⓘ` = *Show the Project* (`temcoProjects.showProject`) → the Documentation panel comes forward at its **Project** section.

## Documentation — four views, the tree first

```
 DOCUMENTATION · T3000 Documentation          ＋  🗀  ⇩  📷  ⎘  ⟳  ⋯
 ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ ▾ T3000Webview-EEZ-Studio            318 · folder              │
 │   ▾ t3000                            142                       │
 │     ▾ bacnet-api                       3                       │
 │         Screen JSON — Format Reference      stale · no 📷      │
 │         Point Types                         never reviewed     │
 │         Trend Logs                          ✓                  │
 │     ▾ data-points                      5                       │
 │     ▸ features                        10                       │
 │   ▾ legacy                            12                       │
 │ ▾ T3000 Docs Mirror                  128 · read from GitHub    │
 │ ▸ T3000 Legacy Archive                 — · no local copy [ ⇩ ] │
 ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ PROJECT                                                        │
 │   Sources  3                                                   │
 │   Outputs  Site · Offline help                                 │
 ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ PIPELINE                                                       │
 │   ⚑ Review Center      2 drafts waiting                  →     │
 │   ▶ Build Center       site ✓ 12:04 · help ✗             →     │
 │   ⬆ Releases           13.0 published                    →     │
 ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │ WORKING SET (6)                                                │
 │   data-points/scaling-and-engineering-units                    │
 └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Title bar: `＋` New Page · `🗀` New Chapter · `⇩` Add a Source · `📷` Capture · `⎘` Paste · `⟳` Refresh.

## The page and the right panel

```
 ┌ DOCUMENTATION ────┬ Data Points API.md ──────────────────┬ DRAFT ────────────────┐
 │ ▾ t3000           │ [ B  I  H2  •  ❝  </>  🔗  📷 ]      │  Draft from…          │
 │   ▾ bacnet-api    │                                      │   ◉ a source file     │
 │       Screen…     │ # Data Points API                    │   ○ another page      │
 │       Point…      │                                      │   ○ a commit range    │
 │ PROJECT           │ Points carry a scale factor.         │ ────────────────────  │
 │   Sources 3       │                                      │  Model · prompt v3    │
 │ PIPELINE          │ ![Trend log setup](media/02-…)       │  [ Draft the Page ]   │
 │   Review · Build  │ 1 Trend log list  2 Period  3 Filter │  Result (diff)        │
 │ WORKING SET (6)   │                                      │  [ Apply ][ Reject ]  │
 └───────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────┘
```

**The rule:** the right panel holds what you consult while working in the page — **DRAFT** for a documentation page, **SCHEMA** for a SQL query. Everywhere else it is closed.

## What the page is, for every row

| Left panel row | Page in the middle |
|---|---|
| a project row | the **project page** — counts, sources, outputs, build buttons |
| Overview · a fact row | the page it counts: tree · report · Problems · project form · Build Center |
| Needs You · a group | the **queue page** |
| Needs You · a capture | the attach prompt: which page takes it |
| Needs You · retake / never reviewed | the page, with its image |
| Recent activity · a row | the activity page |
| a tree page | the page, in the editor, DRAFT on the right |
| Project · a source | that source's page |
| Project · Outputs | the project's edit form |
| Pipeline · Review / Build / Releases | Review Center · Build Center · Releases |
| a Working Set row | the page |
| a repository row | the repository page |
| a model or a waiting draft | the AI page |
| a SQL table row | SQL, with the statement filled in |

## The flow

```
 ①  DASHBOARD · PROJECTS  ＋  → name · source (folder) · output (site)
        └─ the row appears, selected
             ├─ [ ✎ ] → DOCUMENTATION · tree
             └─ [ ⓘ ] → DOCUMENTATION · project + pipeline
 ②  DOCUMENTATION · ＋ New Page → location · kind · title → one file
        ├─ the row lands in the tree; F2 renames it
        ├─ [ Run the generator ] → it becomes a page
        └─ the page opens; Ctrl+Alt+S / Ctrl+Alt+V put a screen in its media/
 ③  DASHBOARD · Recent activity shows the build, the capture, the review
 ④  DOCUMENTATION · PIPELINE → Review Center · Build Center · Releases
```

## Two decisions worth naming

**A row's F2 renames the *file*, and keeps the old id as an alias.** The page id *is* the path (`05-content-and-registry.md`, rule 1), so the rename is the named act that rule allows: the file moves, the repository's own `docs:registry` writes the new entry, and the old id is written into that entry's `aliases` before anything else happens. Shift+F2 renames the title instead, which is the registry value. A project with no generator is refused — without one there is no entry to carry the alias, so the rename would orphan every inbound link.

**The right-hand panel is `DRAFT`.** It lives in the auxiliary bar and is about the page in front: *Draft a Change* asks what the model may read (this page's history · a source file · another page), and the drafts waiting for that page are applied or rejected there — the same commands the Review Center runs.

**Needs You replaced the workbench's Needs Work view.** They were the same question asked twice; the queue has real rows (captures with no page, stale pages, never reviewed) and the old view had one link.

## Still open

- **Annotation**, which waits on the callout decision in `authoring-flow.md` (a webview's DOM, Windows UI Automation, or by hand).
- A **`version-report.json` writer** in the product repository's release workflow: this application reads the file, and shows its pages on the project page and in **Needs You**, but nothing writes it yet.


---

---
title: "The application window"
order: 401
edit: false
---

What the extension looks like inside **Temco Platform** — the shell's panels are unmodified, and only
two of the five areas are ours.

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Temco Platform  -  T3000Webview-EEZ-Studio                                           │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│   │ CONTENT             │ data-points.md              Preview │ TEMCO AI          (local AI) │
│   ├─────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────┤
│ ▣ │ ▾ t3000             │ 1  # Data Points API                │ You   summarise this page    │
│   │   ▾ api-reference   │ 2                                   │ AI    This page lists the API│
│   │     Data Points API │ 3  Read and write the data points   │       points; 14 months old. │
│   │     Database Mgmt   │ 4  of a controller. Each point has a│       Two points are missing.│
│   │   ▾ trends          │ 5  type, a unit and a scaling.      │                              │
│   │ ! Gaps              │ 6                                   │ [ Draft update ] [ Ask area ]│
│   │                     │ 7  Point . Type . Unit . Scaling    │ local  qwen2.5-3b  -  offline│
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ PROBLEMS   OUTPUT   TERMINAL                                                                 │
│ !  data-points.md   Order 20 is used by more than one page in "api-reference".               │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│   335 pages - 12 issues - 341 never reviewed         Markdown  -  Ln 3, Col 1  -  UTF-8      │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

| Panel | What it is | Whose |
|---|---|---|
| **▣ Content** | the docs tree: areas → chapters → pages, counts on the right, **Gaps** at the bottom | ours — the only view that does not exist in ordinary VS Code |
| **Editor** | the page: plain Markdown, no front matter, Preview beside it | the shell's |
| **Temco Docs** | the AI: chat about the open page, `Draft update`, `Ask the area`, and which model is loaded | ours |
| **Problems** | structural faults only, each naming the file to fix | ours |
| **Status bar** | `pages · issues · never reviewed` | mixed |

Three properties of this layout are decisions, not accidents:

1. **The only new real estate is the Content view and the AI panel.** Search, source control, compare,
   terminals, Markdown preview and the problem list are the shell's, already there, nothing to build.
2. **The AI is a panel beside the page, not a separate application.** It reads the page you have open and the
   registry; `Draft update` writes a normal edit you review — so accepting is a diff, rejecting is `Ctrl+Z`.
3. **Both of the module's areas exist now.** The Content view came first and the AI panel after it, and the mock above
   is the panel as built — same place, same buttons, so the layout never had to move.

## Two worlds, and the seam between them

**The dashboard is not in this application.** `00-architecture.md` puts it on the **site**: a **generated
static page** at `/_admin/` — coverage · stale by owner · docs-owed · unowned pages · broken links ·
feedback · help-id coverage — noindex, excluded from the PDF, the help file and the offline copy, and produced
by the **product repository's** own pipeline (the admin report task in `13-implementation-plan.md`). Nothing in
this extension generates it, and nothing here writes into it: the design's rule is that those are *generated
pages, not documentation*.

What the extension does in that relationship is be the **hand-off target**. A dashboard row deep-links into the
editor (`vscode://file/…`), and this is where the page gets fixed:

```
┌── PRODUCT REPO ── the content lives here ──┐    ┌── THE EDITOR ── Temco Platform / plain VS Code ──┐
│                                            │    │                                                          │
│  docs/t3000/**            the pages         │    │  ▣ Temco Docs            (activity bar, ours)       │
│  docs/help/registry.json  the structure     │    │   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │
│  scripts/docs-*.mjs       the generators    │    │   │ CONTENT                                          │   │
│                                            │    │   │  ▾ t3000                                         │   │
│ ┌─ TEMCO LEARN — the site (ships) ───────┐ │    │   │    ▾ api-reference                               │   │
│ │ /t3000/13.0/…   everything published   │ │    │   │      Data Points API   (wrong order)             │   │
│ │ /feed/*.xml      changelog + releases  │ │    │   │        └ why: Order 20 already used              │   │
│ │ downloads/      PDF · help file · zip  │ │    │   │    ▾ trends                                      │   │
│ │ /t3000/13.0/    a pinned version       │ │    │   │  ! Gaps (12)                                     │   │
│ └────────────────────────────────────────┘ │    │   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
│                                            │    │                                                          │
│ ┌─ AUTHORING SURFACE (never ships) ──────┐ │    │   ┌─ right side ──────────────────────────────────────┐   │
│ │ :8080   docfx preview — authoring only │ │    │   │ TEMCO AI                               (the AI)    │   │
│ │                                        │ │    │   │ You  summarise this page                          │   │
│ │ /_admin/         GENERATED REPORT       │ │    │   │ AI   This page lists the API points; 14 months    │   │
│ │   coverage · stale by owner ·          │ │    │   │      old. Two points are missing.                 │   │
│ │   docs-owed · unowned · unreviewed ·   │ │    │   │ [ Draft update ] [ Ask the area ] [ Models… ]     │   │
│ │   broken links · feedback ·            │ │    │   └───────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
│ │   help-id coverage · health score      │ │    │   ┌─ bottom panel ────────────────────────────────────┐   │
│ │   noindex · out of PDF/CHM/zip         │ │    │   │ PROBLEMS  OUTPUT  TERMINAL                        │   │
│ │                                        │ │    │   │  ! data-points.md   Order 20 is used twice        │   │
│ │   each row  ──►  vscode://file/…  ─────┼─┼────┼──►  the page opens in the editor, ready to fix           │
│ │   each row  ──►  the hosted editor       │ │    │   └───────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │
│ │                  (served by the tool)  │ │    │                                                          │
│ └────────────────────────────────────────┘ │    │   status bar:  335 pages · 12 issues · 341 never reviewed │
│                                            │    │                                                          │
│                                            │    │   Build the Site · Build the Offline Help                │
│                                            │    │     └── invoke the *product repo's own* scripts          │
└────────────────────────────────────────────┘    └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

| | |
|---|---|
| **The dashboard** answers *"which pages need work across the organisation?"* — on the site, generated by the product repo, page-level health, never shipped | |
| **The Content view** answers *"which pages need work in the repository I have open?"* — in the editor, one repository, the reasons as children of the page that has them | |
| **The same pages, two distances.** Neither is a copy of the other, and neither recomputes what the other owns | |
| **This extension is where the work happens** — a dashboard row is a hand-off, not a destination | |

*Start a Registry* appears where it applies rather than in a title bar: as the Content view's empty state, for
the folder that has no `docs/help/registry.json` yet. It works on a folder **you have open** — a repository you
have not cloned is refused in words, because starting one there would be a commit made through the API on your
behalf. Filling the registry it creates is the repository's own generator's job (`npm run docs:registry`).

**Where the AI sits, and why that is a user action.** It belongs on the **right**, beside the page being worked
on. VS Code has no contribution point that places a container there — `contributes.viewsContainers` accepts
`activitybar` and `panel` only — so the view is contributed to the panel container and the **workbench docks it
on the right**: the secondary side bar accepts any view, remembers the position, and a fresh install needs one
drag. If it must open there by itself, the alternative is an editor-area webview panel opened beside the page
(`ViewColumn.Beside`), which is a tab rather than a dock. Recorded in `platform-design.md`, not hidden here.

---
title: "The application: shell, screens and flow"
order: 402
edit: false
---

The record of the agreed shape of Temco Platform as an application — the regions of the window, the screens, the way one
leads to the next, and the two rules the design is built on. It is the design of record: a surface that disagrees with it
is either a bug or a proposal to change this file.

Confirmed 2026-10-27. Decisions and their reasons are in *What was decided*; the work order is in *Order of work*.

To **use** the application rather than design it, read [getting-started.md](../getting-started): the same flow as numbered steps, with the button for every page and the command id behind it.

## The two rules

1. **A VS Code view is native.** The workbench draws a tree view; an extension cannot put Fluent inside one. So the left
   panel is a Fluent **header + chips + footer** (a webview view) above a **native Pages tree**. The tree keeps reveal,
   drag, multi-select, keyboard and accessibility for free — the parts where re-implementing costs the most and shows the
   least.
2. **One project is one window state.** Switching project re-reads and lands on the dashboard. Nothing keeps describing
   the project that was open before.

## The regions

```
┌────┬───────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────┐
│ A  │  LEFT PANEL       │  EDITOR AREA (tabs)                       │  RIGHT PANEL     │
│ C  │  project card     │  dashboards · forms · the page editor     │  Outline         │
│ T  │  search           │                                           │  Draft           │
│ I  │  status chips     │                                           │  Properties      │
│ V  │  pages tree       │                                           │                  │
│ I  │  build/release    │                                           │                  │
│ T  │                   │                                           │                  │
│ Y  │                   │                                           │                  │
├────┴───────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────┤
│ OUTPUT · PROBLEMS · TERMINAL        build log · registry diagnostics · the repo's tasks│
├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ Contoso T3000 · 2 sources · 148 pages · 3 stale · v1.4 · Build ✓ 10:42 · Pages ✓        │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## Activity bar: five entries

| | entry | contains | today's views |
|---|---|---|---|
| ◉ | **Home** | projects, recent, what needs you, Learn | `temcoDashboard` (projects, platform, activity) + `temcoLearn` |
| ▤ | **Docs** | the workspace: pages, sources, publish | `temco` (pages, needs-you, sources, publish) + `temcoRepositories.list` |
| ✦ | **AI** | chats, models, drafts | `temcoAiHub` + the `temcoAi` secondary sidebar |
| ⚙ | **Tools** | SQL Studio, and the modules not written yet | `temcoSql` |
| ⚙ | **Settings** | settings, Help | `temcoSettings` + `temcoHelp` |

Eight icons became five: three are places to *be* (Home, Docs, AI), two are where you *configure* things. `Repositories`
moved into Docs ▸ Sources, `Learn` into Home, `Help` under Settings. No view is deleted.

**Implemented** (2026-10-27): the five containers exist with those titles, `temcoRepositories.list` is contributed under
`temco`, and the `temcoLearn`/`temcoHelp`/`temcoRepos` containers are gone. Container **ids** were kept where they were
already used (`temcoDashboard`, `temco`, `temcoAiHub`, `temcoSql`, `temcoSettings`) so saved layout and `onView`
activation keep working — only titles and view membership changed. The guard in `build/check-temco-modules.ts` now refuses
a view pointed at a container nobody declares, which is the mistake this consolidation can make silently.

## Screens

| # | screen | center | left | right |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Home — no project** | hero: New project · Open a source · recent | projects tree | — |
| S2 | **Create project** | 3-step form: name/slug/version → where → what is in it | — | — |
| S3 | **Home — project selected** | one callout for the one thing that needs a person, the five-stage pipeline with the figure each stage is answerable for, then *what needs a person* and *what happened lately* | project tree | — |
| S4 | **Add source** | — | inline form in Sources | — |
| S5 | **Docs workspace** | the page editor or a dashboard tab | **Fluent panel**: project card · search · chips · pages tree · build/release footer | Outline · Draft · Properties |
| S6 | **Registry review** | two panes: areas ↔ pages, with issues, and *Fix* | as S5 | — |
| S7 | **Page editor** | Temco Editor: one-row bar, paper, status strip | as S5 | Outline (default) · Draft · Properties |
| S8 | **New page** | kind · title · area · order · ships → creates file + registry row → **S7** | as S5 | — |
| S9 | **Preview / Diff** | beside the editor: the published layout, or the diff against HEAD | as S5 | — |
| S10 | **Build** | step list (Resolve → Render → Assets → Sitemap), streaming log, result card | as S5 | — |
| S11 | **Release** | version, what ships, destination `releases/<version>/`, a confirmation naming every value | as S5 | — |
| S12 | **Deploy to Pages** | repo · address the site will have · what will be committed · **Publish**; never force-push | as S5 | — |
| S13 | **Export** | format cards: site `.zip` · single-file HTML · Markdown bundle · (`.docx`) · scope | as S5 | — |
| S14 | **AI** | conversations, models, the answer with page citations | conversations + runtime | — |
| S15 | **Learn** | the site: documentation · training · tests · products · Q&A · topics, then the reader for one version | the site folder | — |
| S16 | **Settings** | profile · platform (theme, AI runtime, build defaults, Pages branch) | sections | — |
| S17 | **Help** | search, topics, support (logs, diagnostics) | topics | — |

Screens S1–S13 are the documentation journey; S14–S17 are the platform around it.

## Navigation

| from | action | to |
|---|---|---|
| anywhere, no project | launch | S1 |
| S1 | New project | S2 → S6 |
| S2 | Create | S6; the status bar switches to the new project |
| S5 tree / S3 row | a page | S7 (same tab), split on `Ctrl+click` |
| S6 | a row | S7, or the issue in the page's own Problems |
| S7 | Build | S10 → S11 → S12 |
| S11 | Release | S12 (toast), S10 keeps the result |
| S12 | Deploy | stays; status bar `Pages ✓` and the URL opens |
| S13 | Export | stays; the file is revealed in the OS |
| any | a registry error | S6 with the issue selected |
| any | the project in view is gone | S1 |

## The publish journey

```
create project ─▶ add source ─▶ registry ─▶ write ─▶ validate ─▶ BUILD ─▶ RELEASE ─▶ DEPLOY ─▶ EXPORT
                                    (S6)      (S7)     (S6)      (S10)     (S11)     (S12)    (S13)
```

- **Build** renders the registry itself: `dist/site/<page>.html` from `marked`, one stylesheet, `sitemap.xml` (only when
  the GitHub remote gives a real address), `.nojekyll`. A project that already has its own `docs:site` / `docs:build`
  script keeps using it — the generator is the fallback that makes "a folder of `.md` files" buildable.
- **Release** freezes `releases/<version>/` and records it in the catalog (already built).
- **Deploy** commits the built site to the branch Pages serves and pushes it — a plain, fast-forward push, never a forced
  one: if the branch holds work this build does not know about, that is reported instead of erased. The commit is made in
  a throwaway repository whose only parent is the branch's tip, so the project's own working tree is never touched.
  The one-time Pages switch (`Settings → Pages → Source: branch “gh-pages”, folder “/”`) is attempted through the `gh` CLI
  when it is installed, and printed as two clicks when it is not.
  **Why not Actions:** the generator reads the documentation registry, which only exists inside this extension, so a CI job
  cannot run it. An Actions workflow would need a second copy of the renderer inside the project — a copy that goes stale
  the first time either side changes. CI publishing becomes possible again the day a project owns its own renderer; the
  branch push is what makes publishing work until then.
- **Export** is local and dependency-free: the site as a `.zip`, any page as one self-contained HTML file, the pages and
  their media as a Markdown bundle. The zip writer is in the module (`src/zip.ts`, store + deflate over a CRC32 table)
  rather than a dependency, because an export a colleague cannot open is not an export. `.docx` needs a writer package
  (`mammoth` is import-only) and is still owed.

## Temco Learn

Temco Learn is its own module (`extensions/temco-learn`, the tenth), because a reader-facing website is not the same job as
keeping a page in step with the code. **The website itself is a separate repository** — `temcocontrols/temco-learn`, Vite ·
React 19 · Fluent UI v9 · Markdown — and this module is a *client* of that checkout: it runs the site's own servers, builds it,
shows it, writes new builds into it, and commits the result. Nothing about rendering the site lives here any more, so the site
can be worked on, built and published by someone who never opens the editor.

`temcoLearn.siteRepo` names the clone (empty means `~/Temco/temco-learn`). Everything below is a fact about that folder:

- `content/<project>/site.json` — the project's title, section and summary; `content/<project>/<version>/*.md` — the pages a
  person writes, with front matter for the title;
- `public/imported/<project>/<version>/` — a project's own build HTML, copied in as it came out;
- `public/catalog.json` — the one file both the website and this module read. `npm run catalog` folds the two sources above
  into it (order, titles, versions, `builtAt`), so the panel cannot list a page the site does not have. A second reader of a
  second shape is how a panel and a page start disagreeing;
- `package.json` scripts — `client-dev` (Vite, with reload), `build` (catalog + search index + `tsc` + `vite build`), `serve`
  (the built pages), `catalog`, `search`, `check`. Neither server script names a port: the module passes `--port`, so there
  is one port for both and one setting that moves it;
- `.github/workflows/pages.yml` — a push at `main` builds the site and hands `dist` to GitHub Pages. The built site is never
  committed: what is published is the source and the catalog it generates.

**The module runs the site, it does not re-implement it.** `src/siteRunner.ts` spawns the repository's own `serve` script
through `npm`, tracks one server on one port (1025 by default), and treats "starting" as distinct from "running": it polls
the address until it answers, so a frame is never pointed at a port with nothing behind it. `Stop` kills the process tree and
waits for the port to go quiet, because `npm` hands the server to a child that can outlive the kill. A `dist` that was never
built is built before the built pages are first served, rather than served as an empty folder.

**`Preview` is a panel framing the running site** (`src/preview.ts`, `temcoLearn.site.preview`), with a bar over the frame
that is a **caption, not a toolbar**: a tinted strip (`--vscode-sideBar-background`, 34px, one hairline under it) holding the
state as a coloured dot and one word, then the address, then the acts — and every one of those is a **link**, not a box, so
nothing in the strip competes with the website below it. No address bar, because the address is not a question: one port, set
once, and a person who wants the site is not choosing a URL — but the address *is* clickable, because what a person does with
a URL is click it, and it goes where the act beside it goes. The three acts read `Reload · Open in Browser · Stop`: the second
one is named for where it goes rather than for the noun `Browser`, which said what the thing is instead of what the button
does. `Reload` and `Open in Browser` are inert with no address; `Stop` never is. Closing the panel does not stop the server, the
way closing a browser tab does not stop a website; `Stop` does. `Add This Build to the Site` copies a build into
`public/imported/<project>/<version>/`, writes the `site.json` the catalog needs (the title comes from the build's own
`<title>`), runs `npm run catalog`, and shows the new version — only the version is asked, and it is asked once, in the
version box, never in a notification. `Publish` commits the clone and pushes it at `main`; it refuses a folder that is not a
clone instead of initialising one, because an empty history pushed at a real site is worse than a refusal.

The panel is **three views in the container**, the way Home is three — VS Code draws the header, the separator and the
collapse state, so the grouping is the one a person already knows from the Explorer, and each section carries its own
title-bar actions. A row is a name and a glyph, and what it holds is `TreeItem.description` — the grey words the Explorer
writes beside a folder (`3 versions`), never a badge of our own. Sentences live on the Overview page and in tooltips, never in
a row.

| view | rows | the grey words |
|---|---|---|
| **Overview** | one row, `Temco Learn`, which opens the page | — |
| **Site** | **Preview the Site** (a state dot and one word; its heading opens the Site page; `Stop`/`Start`, `Open` and `Open in Browser :1025` under it), then **Library** (one row per project, at its newest version) | the state: `Running`, `Starting…`, `Stopped`, `Failed`, `Not set up`; projects served |
| **Artifacts** | one row per project built on this machine, opening onto the files its build wrote; `Publish` closes the view (`initialSize: 4`) | **nothing** — the name only, and a hover carries the count and the paths; `Publish` puts its count at the row’s right end |

**Three views, one subject each.** *Artifacts* is **content**: the files the builds on this machine wrote, plus the single
act that is about all of them at once (`Publish`). Nothing here is a task list and nothing here is a state — a view holds
things, and the things are files.

**Adding a build is not in the panel at all**, because it is a *site* operation: it copies a build into the site repository
and asks for its version, exactly like cloning, building and publishing write into that repository. So it lives on the **Site
page**, as a section of its own — `Builds not on the site`, one row per build with the reason it waits (`not on the site`,
`newer than 13.0`) and one `Add…` button — and every command that means "add a build" (`temcoLearn.addBuilds`, from the
Overview callout, its workflow card, the Publishing page's step, the `+` on the Site view) opens that page **with the section
first and marked**. That is the whole answer to "what is that row for": it is the step that turns a folder on disk into a
version on the website, it needs the version typed, and it belongs beside the other steps on the site.

`Build a project` is not a row either: building happens in Temco Docs, so it appears **only when nothing has been built at
all** — `Nothing built on this machine yet` / `Build a Project…` — where it is the honest next step. The ellipsis is VS Code's
signal that a click leaves this window, and the tooltip says where it goes.

A label is **navigation** or an **instruction**, and the two are not the same sentence: a button on a page that goes to the
other module reads `Open Temco Docs` (the way VS Code says `Open Settings`), while the last row of an empty view reads
`Build a Project…`, because a view with one line in it has to answer *what do I do*, not *what opens*.

**Site** answers two questions, in that order. **Preview the Site** is a group whose *heading* is the page and whose
*children* are the only acts a panel is good for: `Stop` while it is running and `Start` while it is not (one row, the
word swapping with the state), then the two places the site can be read — `Open`, a tab in this window, and `Open in
Browser`, which shows the port alone (`:1025`) — and both start the server first if it is down. The two are named for the
difference a person feels, not for the API behind them: `Open` stays here, `Open in Browser` leaves for the browser they
already have. Everything else a clone needs — clone, pull, install, build, change the folder or the port, the log — is the Site
page's, because a second copy in the panel is how a panel and a page come to disagree about what is running. A clone that is
not there swaps those rows for `Clone temco-learn…` and `Use an Existing Clone…`, and the heading says `Not set up` and
still opens the page that explains it.

The heading's glyph is the **state**, not the site: a filled circle in `charts.green` while it serves, `sync~spin` while it
comes up, `error` in `charts.red` when it failed, `circle-outline` when it is stopped, `cloud-download` in `charts.blue` when
there is no clone — the way VS Code's own Ports view draws a running process, so a glance down the panel answers *is it up?*
before a word is read.

**A row's facts sit at the row's right-hand end**, and in a tree that is a decoration badge and nothing else: `description`
is drawn inline, immediately after the name, so it can never be a column — but a badge is a `::after` on the label element,
which is a flex row, so it is the last flex item and lands at the edge (`views.css` even gives it a 4px right margin).
`RowDecorations` answers one badge per row of the module's own scheme: `Running`/`Stopped`/`Failed`/`Not set up` in the
state's colour, `:1025` on the browser row, `2 projects` on the library. So the three rows read as a table without a single
space character of padding, and the words are in the tooltips as well, for anyone who has turned badges off. The provider
answers **synchronously**: a decoration that arrives as a promise leaves the first paint of its row blank, because
`DecorationsService` only has the data when it is already there.

**Library** is one row per **project, at its newest version** (`T3000 · 13.0`), opening that page on the site at
`#/<project>/<version>/` and starting the server first if it is stopped. Not one row per version: a product with twenty
versions turned the panel into a scroll bar, the newest is the one a person wants nine times in ten, and the site's own list
holds the rest. The name is `Library` because the rows are documentation sets, not pages — a *page* lives inside a version,
and a *category* is the catalog's `section` field, a different axis. The row writes the project's **name**, without the
"docs" a build's own page title usually ends with (`Test1 docs` reads `Test1 · 1.0`), and it carries no description: the
version is already in the label, and a grey `2 pages` beside four rows was the noisiest thing in the view. A site with
nothing on it does not report emptiness: its one row is `Add a Build to the Site…`, which runs the same picker the view's
title bar does. There is deliberately no group of `dist` builds: the build as written is a file on disk, which is the
Artifacts page's business, not something to read here.

**The Site page is where the clone is managed** (`src/sitePage.ts`, `temcoLearn.site.page`), and it is what `Preview the website`
opens when there is no clone — a folder dialog was never an explanation. It reads the folder every time it is drawn
(`src/siteFacts.ts`: branch, commit, ahead/behind, changed paths, `node_modules`, `dist`) and offers the act for each answer:
clone into `~/temco/temco-learn`, use an existing clone, change the folder or the port, pull (`--ff-only`, never a merge),
install, build, start, stop, open a terminal, and clear or open the log (`src/siteLog.ts`, the same bounded ring the publish
page keeps). The runner grew the two git steps the page needs (`git`, `pull`, `clone`), so a clone and a pull print into the
same log a build and a server do.

The page is a control panel, not an article, and it names itself **`Documentation Site`** — `Site` said nothing the view
title did not, and `Temco Learn` above it was a breadcrumb of one, which is a label pretending to be navigation. The title
block says **what the site is** (its own repository, the toolchain, one port) and the state (`Running · localhost:1025 ·
built`, `Stopped`, `Failed`), then **one primary act chosen by that state** — `Clone` → `Install dependencies` → `Build the
site` → `Start the site` → `Open`, with `Try again` when a start fails. `Open` goes to a tab in this window; the title
carries the `globe` chip, because it is the page of the site and the browser is what it shows.

The site is read in two places, and the page names both the way the panel does: `Open` in the **This machine** row stays
here, `Open in Browser` leaves for the browser the reader already has. A button labelled `Open` that opened a browser was
the ambiguity worth removing; the tooltip on each says which one it is.

A failure is said **once**, at the top, in the tinted callout — with the reason and the two acts that answer it (`Try again`,
`Open the full log`). The title block then says only `Failed`; saying the reason twice made the reader look for the
difference between two identical messages.

The clone's own facts are **not in the sentence**. A meta line under the title block carries them, in mono, and pairs them
with the two figures that say how far the copy has drifted — `0 commits behind origin`, `5 paths changed` — so they scan as
numbers with left edges in a column rather than hiding at the end of a paragraph. The repository, the commit and the changed
paths are **links** (a command, never an `href`): the first two open GitHub at that commit, the last opens the Publishing
page, which is where those paths are listed. A clone with nothing changed links nothing — a link to an empty list is a dead
end:

```
temcocontrols/temco-learn · main · 577ca57 · committed 3 days ago
0 commits behind origin        5 paths changed
```

Below that, the page is two parts, and both section titles wear the brand's vertical rule (the shape the Overview page uses):

| part | what it answers | drawn as |
|---|---|---|
| **This machine** | what is here, and is it running | the server as one line — state in mono, `Repository │ Terminal`, then `Start`/`Stop`, `Open` and `Open in Browser` — then four **small cards in one row**: Folder · Port · Installed · Built (two across under 760px). A card is a label, a value and the act that changes it, **with no border**: its condition is a 6px dot in `charts.green` when the step is done, `charts.yellow` when it is not, and a hollow one when there is no condition to report |
| **Activity** | what it said | a tabbed area, `Output` and `Publish`, plain text on a tint off the card surface: no border, no header bar, no line numbers. The tabs are radios and labels, so they switch with no script and still answer the keyboard |

**`Log` was the wrong word, and so was `Console`** — a log is a file, and this is what the module *did*: a clone, a pull, a
build, a server, and the last publish. It used to be two places as well: pressing `Build` opened the preview panel to report
on itself, so a build either pushed the site out of the way or the message was lost. Every message now goes to the page that
