### Sources — what the platform reads from

```
  KIND     SOURCE                      BRANCH      REGISTRY  DOCS   PAGES  LAST PUSH          READ AS
  git      T3000Webview                main        —         yes    318    main 2 days ago    fetch
    └ registry is on feature/lvgl-svg-renderer, not on main          ⚠ branch mismatch
  git      T3_Engine                   release/2.1 yes       yes     96    rel/2.1 5 months    api · floor
  git      T3000-API                   main        —         —        —     main 3 years       api · floor
  folder   C:\docs\t3000-extra         —           —         yes     34    —                  local
  folder   C:\docs\legacy              —           yes       yes    104    —                  local
  archive  vendor\bacnet-stack.pdf     —           —         —        —     —                  read-only
  … 17 more repositories with no documentation found

  [ Scan for sources ]  [ Add a source… ]  [ Prune the source cache (1.8 GB of 2 GB) ]
```

### Create a document

```
  ┌─ Create a document ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  1  Name        T3000 Developer Help    version 1.4    audience developers    owner docs   │
  │                                                                                           │
  │  2  Sources — pick from what the catalog knows                         [ + Add a source ]  │
  │     [x] T3000Webview    branch [ main ▾ ]    318 pages · registry —        ⚠ see below      │
  │     [x] C:\docs\t3000-extra                  34 pages · no registry                       │
  │     [ ] T3_Engine       branch [ rel/2.1 ▾ ]  96 pages · registry yes                      │
  │       the platform says what it found:                                                    │
  │         the registry is on feature/lvgl-svg-renderer, not main   → [ use that branch ]     │
  │         C:\docs\t3000-extra has 34 pages and no registry         → [ start one ]          │
  │                                                                                           │
  │  3  Recipe      engine [ docfx ▾ ]   config docs/docfx.json                                │
  │     outputs     [x] CHM t3000_dev.chm  [x] PDF t3000_dev.pdf  [ ] publish to the site     │
  │     it runs the repository's own script, as a task — never a re-implementation             │
  │                                                                                           │
  │  4  Review      T3000 Developer Help v1.4 · 2 sources · 352 pages · chm + pdf              │
  │                 first build:  npm run docs:registry  then  npm run docs:site               │
  │                                                     [ Back ]  [ Create ]  [ Create & build]│
  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### Reading a source — three modes

```
  local      a folder you have open, or a path in the catalog
             files · checks · edits · builds                                    cost: none

  fetch      a shallow clone into  globalStorage/sources/<slug>
             git clone --filter=blob:none --depth 1                             cost: disk (2 GB, LRU)
             used for: drafts (the model needs the page text), builds, file-existence checks

  api        one commit's changed files + patches, over the host's API
             used for: commit summaries, "what changed"                         cost: calls (5 000/hour)
             rule: summaries need diffs · drafts need files
```

### AI analysis — the job, and what it produced

```
  AI — analysis over history                                  model qwen2.5-3b · local · offline
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  Job    18 of 24 commits summarised · 6 remaining      [ Run ]  [ Pause ]  [ Run the rest ]
         T3000Webview @ main · since 2026-09-01 · skipped 1 (patch too large: 1.4 MB)

  SUMMARISED
    411c5e55  2 days ago   docs   Clean the docs and move them into the platform repository
                           → moved 3 chapters, rewrote the design hub intro
    f27cf000  5 days ago   docs   Update the docs to add a YAML file for DocFX
                           → affected t3000/architecture (2 pages)

  DRAFTS TO REVIEW
    t3000/architecture/overview.md   +12 −4   "the registry now declares a YAML file…"  [ Open diff ]
    t3000/data-points/README.md      +3  −0   "the Design Studio section moved"         [ Open diff ]

  PAGES OWED   14 pages · oldest 8 months      [ Open the owed list ]

  Nothing here is published or committed: applying a draft is a diff you accept, one `Ctrl+Z` away.
```

### Which page reads what

```
  Documents page   ← documents · pages · builds · artifacts · events
  Sources page     ← sources · scans · pages · commits
  Document page    ← doc_sources · recipes · builds · artifacts · pages · commits
  Create a doc     ← sources (what exists)   → documents · doc_sources · recipes
  AI analysis      ← commits · commit_pages · pages   → summaries · drafts · owed
  Build log        ← builds · artifacts
  Notifications    ← events where seen_at is null
```

### What every surface obeys

- **Say where the facts came from.** Every row carries its fidelity — local, fetch or api · floor.
- **Empty states act.** A page with nothing on it offers the one thing that applies, never a sentence
  telling the reader to go and find it.
- **A problem lands where the fix is** — the page for a page problem, its row for a document that has never
  been built.
- **Nothing is written to a source**, and nothing is published, except by a command the user ran and
  confirmed.

## Decisions

| Decision | Made |
|---|---|
| **The AI's dock** | docked on the **right by the workbench** — the secondary side bar takes any view and remembers the position. No contribution point can place it there, so a fresh install needs one drag, and that drag is the documented setup step |
| **The catalog's store** | **`node:sqlite`** — built into the Node the extension host runs, so the platform adds no dependency for it. Where that built-in is missing, the store refuses in words at the point of use and activation is unaffected |
| **Branch policy** | the default branch, with the mismatch reported and a per-source override |
| **Notification default** | `docs-only` — a daily summary of documentation change, no modals |
| **The entry point** | **the welcome page opens on activation** (`temcoWorkbench.start.open`, default `welcome`, with the checkbox in its own footer as VS Code has it), falling through to the project's pages, then the page you were last on, then the overview. The one recorded project selects itself, so the tree is ready without anyone picking it |
| **The Workspace, gated by content** | **the Workspace stays the first view and the Library sits beneath it**, and the open folders are never read into it. Its empty states are **rows the tree draws itself**, offering the act that fills it — *create a project* · *choose one in the Library* · *open this project and read its sources*. Hiding the view on a context key (`when:`) and phrasing these states as `viewsWelcome` were both tried and rejected: a selected project with no visible tree reads as a fault, and a welcome's `when` is resolved before the refresh has read anything |

## Build order

| Step | What | Done when |
|---|---|---|
| **R1** | **The catalog** — the store, its schema, migrations, and the rows above | **done**: 12 tables, applied idempotently and exercised against a real `node:sqlite` database — inserts, the document↔source join, the unseen-events query. The store lives in `globalStorageUri`, never in a repository |
| **R2** | **Sources** — add a git source (any host, chosen branch) or a folder; local, materialised and online modes | **partly built**: a source is added, read in `local` and `api` mode, and remembered across restarts. Not built: the materialised (`fetch`) mode, and the branch mismatch is reported by the Content view rather than by a read |
| **R3** | **Documents** — create one, cite sources, choose a recipe, and build it | **partly built**: a project is created, cites sources, chooses a recipe, and its build runs the repository's own script as a task and records the files by path. Not verified: a real artifact (`t3000_dev.chm`) has not been produced through this path yet |
| **R4** | **AI over history** — commit summaries, affected pages, drafts, the owed list, as a resumable job | a real commit has a summary and a draft, and a human still approves |
| **R5** | **The views** — Documents and Sources: health, fidelity, latest push, *Since you last looked* | **partly built**: the sidebar holds Workspace (the pages of the project being worked on, with a row saying why while there is nothing) first and Library (projects · sources · repositories · catalog · needs attention · the AI) beneath it, and *What needs doing* answers "what do I have, and what changed?" on a page. Not built: the *Since you last looked* digest, and fidelity per source beyond the read mode |
| **R6** | **Editing and publishing** — the editor with its toolbar, creating a document, images, draft branches, the design package's hosted editor, PR flow | **partly built**: the **toolbar** is built on VS Code's own editor — emphasis, blocks, a link to a real page, the outline — as the sixteen `temcoDocs.format.*` commands in `src/format.ts`. A page can be **created, named, moved and retired** — `src/pageOps.ts`, six commands and a drag controller — and a project's own scripts can be run from its card on the projects page. Not built: inserting an image, drafts and the PR flow, which need the `fetch` read mode and a review flow that lives in the product repository |

**R6 is mostly a rule change, and the rule was changed by naming it.** The toolbar edits the buffer
a person is already typing in, so it adds no writer at all. Creating a page writes **one file** — the repository's
own `docs:registry` adds the entry, derives its place from the path and its title from the first heading, so
nothing here writes an entry for a page that did not exist. And a page that is *moved* changes **one curated value
at a time** in `docs/help/registry.json`, through `src/registryEdit.ts`, which renders the file the way its own
generator does — including the file's line endings — so a move is a two-line diff. `docs/editor-design.md` §5
records the decision, the four rules that hold it, and the evidence.

**R1–R3 make it a documentation platform**: sources in, documents and artifacts out. R4 makes it intelligent.
R5 makes it a management system. R6 closes the loop.

## What this does not change

- **Content never moves.** A document cites sources; it never relocates their pages.
- **Only artifacts ship.** A customer installation receives the CHM and the F1 map — never this app.
- **The AI proposes; a person approves.**
- **The design package still wins** on content, registry and publishing rules. This document only says what the
  platform knows, where it keeps it, and what it builds.


---

---
title: "Docs center: the redesign"
order: 406
edit: false
---

One consolidated design for the documentation center. It supersedes the layout sections of `app-flow.md`, `authoring-flow.md` and `extension-design.md`, which contradict each other. Nothing here is built yet.

## 1. The model

```
 Sources ─► Temco Platform (authoring) ─► Product package ─► Temco Learn (website)
 repos, pdf,   projects · AI · review       products/<id>/       search · downloads · versions
 word, chm                                  manifest.json
                                            docs/ api/ guides/ assets/ releases/
```

| System | Job | Where |
|---|---|---|
| **Temco Platform** | where people work: projects, sources, AI drafts, review, generate | this repository |
| **Product package** | the unit of exchange; a plug-in the portal discovers | `products/<id>/` |
| **Temco Learn** | the reader's MSDN-style portal | the `temco-learn` repository |
| **DocFX** | one pluggable build engine; CHM and PDF are other exporters | invoked by Build, never a surface |

**Vocabulary.** Project (a `documents` row) → Section (the registry's *area*) → Folder (its *chapter*, nestable) → Page. Versions come from the project's releases. A `products` table is added only if several versions must be open at once.

**Truth rule.** Markdown and `registry.json` in the repository are the content. `catalog.sqlite` is a register (projects, sources, builds, releases, drafts, caches) and never holds a second copy of page text, except a draft not yet accepted.

## 2. Journey

```
 A LAUNCH ─► B START PAGE ─► C CREATE PROJECT ─► D EMPTY PROJECT ─► E ADD PAGES
                                                                        │
   I RELEASE ◄─ H PUBLISH ◄─ G NEEDS YOU / AI LOOP ◄─ F EDIT / CAPTURE ◄┘
```

### A. First launch

```
┌────┬───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ▤◀ │ DOCS                      │  Welcome to Temco Docs                                       │
│    │  No project yet           │   ┌─ Start ─────────────────┐  ┌─ Bring in existing ───────┐ │
│    │  [ ＋ Create a project ]  │   │ ＋ New project          │  │ Word · RTF · CHM · HTML   │ │
│    │  [ ⇩ Import a doc set  ]  │   │ ▤ From a template       │  │ A folder of Markdown      │ │
│    │  [ ⎇ Open a repository ]  │   │ ⎇ From a repository     │  │ A git repository          │ │
│    │                           │   └─────────────────────────┘  └───────────────────────────┘ │
├────┴───────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ catalog.sqlite created · default organisation written                                         │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### B. Start page (projects exist; shown in the editor, not a container)

```
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ Temco Docs                                                      ＋ New project │
│ RECENT PROJECTS                                                               │
│  ▤ T3000 Documentation   v13.0   3 sources   built 12:04 ✓   [ Open ] [ ⋯ ]   │
│  ▤ BACnet Tools          v1.2    1 source    never built     [ Open ] [ ⋯ ]   │
│ NEEDS YOU (all projects)                 RECENT ACTIVITY                      │
│  ⚑ 2 drafts waiting       T3000          12:04 build site ✓ T3000             │
│  📷 1 screenshot to place  T3000          11:40 capture placed → Scaling       │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### C. Create a project (4 steps)

```
 1 ORIGIN   ◉ Blank · ○ From a template · ○ From a repository · ○ Import a document set · ○ Open existing
 2 BASICS   Name [T3000 Documentation]  Version [13.0]  Owner [alice ▾]  Audience [developer ▾]
 3 SOURCES  ☑ ⎇ t3000webview [main ▾] [src/**]   ☑ ⎇ t3000-esp [main ▾]   ＋ GitHub org · folder · archive
 4 OUTPUTS  Engine [DocFX ▾]  ☑ Website ☑ PDF ☑ CHM ☑ Search index ☐ AI index  →  Review  →  [ Create ]
```

Create writes `documents`, `doc_sources`, `recipes` and the smallest valid `docs/help/registry.json`. The six default sections are seeded for a blank project: Overview, Guides, Architecture, API, Troubleshooting, Release Notes.

### D. First open (empty project)

```
┌────┬───────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ▤◀ │ DOCS                ＋ 🗀 ⟳│ T3000 Documentation 13.0                                 │
│    │ ▾ T3000 Docs · 13.0       │  Your project is ready. Next:                            │
│    │     (empty)               │   ① Add the first page            [ New Page ]           │
│    │ ▸ NEEDS YOU          (0)  │   ② Bring in existing material     [ Import ]            │
│    │ ▸ SOURCES            (2)  │   ③ Let the AI draft from sources  [ Draft sections ]    │
│    │ ▸ PUBLISH                 │                                                          │
└────┴───────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## 3. The window

```
┌────┬─────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────┐
│ ▤◀ │ DOCS                  ＋ 🗀 ⟳│ first-project.md × │ Preview │ ASSIST                   │
│    │ ▾ T3000 Docs · 13.0  (pick) │                              │ [Draft][Properties]      │
│    │   ▾ Overview            3   │ markdown editor              │  title · owner · ship    │
│    │   ▾ Guides             14   │  Ctrl+Alt+S inserts image    │  keywords · help ids     │
│    │     ▾ Getting Started   4   │                              │  code · reviewed         │
│    │         First project  ●    │                              │                          │
│    │   ▸ API · Troubleshooting…  │                              │                          │
│    │ ▸ NEEDS YOU (7)  collapsed  │                              │                          │
│    │ ▸ SOURCES (3)    collapsed  │                              │                          │
│    │ ▸ PUBLISH        collapsed  │                              │                          │
├────┴─────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────┤
│ T3000 13.0 · 318 pages · 14 stale · 0 problems         ⚑ 2 drafts        site ✓ · help ✗    │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Constraints from the VS Code API that shape this layout:

- A tree view cannot hold dropdowns, so **the project is the tree's first row**. Clicking it opens a quick-pick; the view title shows it too.
- The three lower views are independent panes, collapsed by default. There is no accordion behaviour.
- A text editor has no tabs: Edit and Preview use the Markdown side-by-side preview, and **Properties is a tab in the right panel**.
- Dropping on the tree reports only the row dropped on, never the gap between rows.
- The tree is **path-based**. A page id is its path under `docs/`, so `guides/getting-started/install.md` is the page `install` in the folder `getting-started`, inside `guides`. Every folder level is derived from those paths: the registry does not have to name the middle folders, and folders nest to any depth.

## 4. E. The Pages tree

### Rows and hover icons

```
 PAGES                                            ＋  🗀  ⇩  ⟳  ⋯
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ ▾ Guides                                    14                     │
│   ▾ Getting Started                  [＋][🗀][⋯]     ← folder hover │
│       Install                                                      │
│       First project   ●              [📷][✦][⋯]      ← page hover   │
│       Licensing                                                    │
│ ▾ API                                                              │
│   ▾ Data Points API                                                │
│       Scaling         ⚠  📷                                         │
│         └ ⚠ code changed since last review                         │
│         └ 📷 no image on this page                                 │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
 Folder row: name and page count only. Leaf row: title only.
 State is a badge (● draft · ⚠ stale · 📷 no image) or a folded child, never an extra row.
 Hover icons: folder = ＋ New Page · 🗀 New Folder · ⋯ ;  page = 📷 Capture · ✦ Draft with AI · ⋯
 Tooltip: id · owner · audience · last change.
```

### New page and new folder: the selection is the location

```
 Selected row            New Page lands…            New Folder lands…
 ─────────────────────   ────────────────────────   ──────────────────────
 nothing / empty space   at the root of the section the root
 a folder                inside it                  inside it (a sub-folder)
 a page                  next to it                 next to it
```

```
 click ＋ (or Ctrl+N with the tree focused)
 ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ New page in  Guides / Getting Started                                  │
 │ [ First project________________________ ]                              │
 │   → getting-started/first-project.md                                   │
 │                              Enter = create    Esc = cancel            │
 └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
          │ Enter ─► file written, registry entry added, row selected, page opens
```

| Act | You type | Automatic |
|---|---|---|
| New Page | title | file name from the slug, kind Article, last position, registry entry, empty template |
| New Page ▸ Task / Reference / Troubleshooting / Release Note | title | the same, with the kind set |
| New Folder | folder name | the folder and its first page, since an empty chapter does not exist in the registry |

The input box is a quick-input, the closest thing the extension API allows to Explorer's inline edit. Renaming in the row itself would need a change to the core of this fork and is deferred.

### Context menus

```
 on a FOLDER row             on a PAGE row                    on empty space
┌─────────────────────┐    ┌───────────────────────────┐    ┌─────────────────┐
│ New Page…           │    │ Open · Open to the Side   │    │ New Page…       │
│ New Folder…         │    │ ───────────────────────── │    │ New Folder…     │
│ Import Here…        │    │ New Page…  (sibling)      │    │ Import…         │
│ ─────────────────── │    │ New Page of a Kind  ▸     │    │ Refresh         │
│ Rename…       F2    │    │ ───────────────────────── │    └─────────────────┘
│ Move Up / Down      │    │ Rename File…       F2     │
│ ─────────────────── │    │ Rename Title…  Shift+F2   │
│ Reveal · Copy Path  │    │ Move Up / Down            │
│ Delete…             │    │ ───────────────────────── │
└─────────────────────┘    │ Capture…  Ctrl+Alt+S      │
                           │ Paste Image  Ctrl+Alt+V   │
                           │ Draft a Change with AI…   │
                           │ Mark Reviewed             │
                           │ ───────────────────────── │
                           │ Reveal · Copy Path        │
                           │ Delete…                   │
                           └───────────────────────────┘
```

### Keys

```
 F2 rename file (old id kept as an alias)    Shift+F2 rename title only    Delete  with confirmation
 Ctrl+C / Ctrl+X / Ctrl+V  copy or move rows between folders      Enter / click  open the page
```

F2 moves the file, the repository's own generator writes the new registry entry, and the old id is written into `aliases` first. A project with no generator is refused, because the rename would orphan inbound links.

### Drag and drop

```
 drag a page   onto a FOLDER row  → into that folder, last position
               onto a PAGE row    → into that page's folder, right after it
               onto empty space   → to the section root
 multi-select  Ctrl/Shift+click, then drag together          Ctrl+drag  copies
 every move    → file moves, `order` rewritten, old id kept as an alias
 refused       → into itself or its own child; across projects
 from the OS   → a .md, .png or .docx dropped on a folder is imported there
```

## 5. F. Editing a page

```
┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────┐
│ first-project.md        [ side-by-side Preview ]              │ ASSIST           │
│ # First project                                               │ [Draft][Ask][Props]
│ Points carry a scale factor.                                  │ Draft from…      │
│ ![Trend log](media/02-trend.png)  ◄─ Ctrl+Alt+S inserts this  │  ◉ source file   │
│                                                               │  ○ another page  │
│                                                               │ RESULT (diff)    │
│                                                               │ [Apply][Reject]  │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────┘
 Capture: snip overlay → clipboard → media/NN-slug.png (warn < 1600 px, refuse > 2 MB).
 No page in front → .temco/captures/ and a row in NEEDS YOU until it is attached.
 Apply is one undoable edit. Drafts wait in SQLite until accepted.
```

The **Properties** tab is the form over the registry fields: title, section, order, owner, audience, ship, keywords, help ids, code, reviewed.

## 6. G. Needs You and the AI loop

```
 NEEDS YOU (7)
  📷 Screenshots to place (1)  row → "which page takes it?"
  ⚠ Retake these (4)          row → the page
  ○ Never reviewed (2)        row → the report
  ● Drafts waiting (2)        row → diff, Apply / Reject

 source changes ─► commit summarised once (model recorded) ─► mapped to pages (code-glob · path · terms)
   ─► page marked stale ─► NEEDS YOU ─► Draft a Change ─► draft row ─► diff ─► Apply
```

## 7. H. Publish

```
┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ PUBLISH · T3000 Documentation 13.0                                 │
│  ① REVIEW ───► ② BUILD ───► ③ RELEASE                              │
│  2 waiting     site ✓ help ✗   not released                        │
│ ① drafts and changed pages: [Open diff] [Approve]                  │
│ ② Engine [DocFX ▾] ☑Website ☑PDF ☑CHM ☐ZIP   [▶ Build]             │
│    success is the artifact left behind, never the exit code        │
│ ③ Writes products/t3000/releases/13.0, latest → 13.0   [⬆ Release] │
└────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

## 8. I. Result on disk, and Temco Learn

```
 products/t3000/
   manifest.json   {id, title, version}
   docs/ guides/ api/ assets/ toc.yml docfx.json
   releases/13.0 · 12.9 · 12.8      latest → 13.0
                    │
                    ▼
 Temco Learn discovers products/*  →  Home · Product page · Doc page · Downloads (Online · PDF · CHM · ZIP)
```

The Learn page's top navigation mirrors the project's sections, so what the writer sees in the tree is what the reader sees.

## 9. SQLite storage (catalog.sqlite)

```
 organisations ─┬─< repositories           cache of what GitHub listed
                │
 sources ───────┤  kind git|folder|archive · remote_url · path · branch · registry_path · last_commit
   │            └──< scans
   ├──< commits ──< commit_pages           sha · summary · summary_model · how: code-glob|path|terms
   │ n..n (doc_sources: branch, include_glob, role)
   ▼
 documents  (the PROJECT)  name · slug · version · audience · owner · origin · template
   ├── 1:1 ─ recipes          engine · config_path · script · outputs
   ├──< pages                 id · path · title · owner · reviewed_at · stale · counts   (cache of registry.json)
   ├──< builds ──< artifacts  status · revision · log_path  /  kind · path · bytes · sha256
   ├──< releases              version · destination · files · published_at · build_id
   └──< drafts                page_id · title · body · state draft|accepted|discarded
 events  at · kind · subject · detail · seen_at       meta  key/value schema version
```

## 10. What the extension does now

The rule this table records: **one list, one owner.** Every kind of list has exactly one surface, and a webview page never lists something a tree or a panel already lists. Everything a webview page *did* became a command on that surface.

| Then | Now |
|---|---|
| views Pages, Project, Pipeline, Working Set, plus the queue declared in `temco-workbench` | Pages, Needs You, Sources, Publish — all declared by `temco-docs` |
| 3-step New Page wizard | the selection is the location; one title box |
| 10 webview pages (projects, project, needs, repository, documents, page, source, review, builds, releases) | 2 left: **Project home** and **Release report**. The other eight commands still exist and now focus the surface that owns the list, so every button and menu item in the other modules still lands somewhere |
| the tree stopped at area → chapter → page | the tree is a path: folders inside folders, any depth, like Explorer |
| Working Set | removed (use Open Editors) |
| Properties as an editor panel | the right panel's **Properties** tab, beside **Draft** |
| publishing copied artifacts | publishing also writes `manifest.json` per product and regenerates the Learn portal |
| `home.ts`, `views.ts` named in `extension-design.md` | do not exist; that document now points here |

## 11. Where the work stands

Done:

1. The three older documents carry a pointer here instead of a competing layout.
2. The four views, the queue moved out of `temco-workbench`, `Sources`/`Publish` renamed.
3. The project is the tree's first row; a new project is seeded with the six sections.
4. New Page and New Folder take their location from the selection; hover icons; multi-select drag and drop.
5. Properties is a tab in the right panel.
6. `manifest.json` is written on publish, and the Learn portal is generated from every product's manifest.

Not done, and deliberately so:

- The `temco-learn` site itself. The portal is generated by this extension and served from local storage; a real reader site is a separate repository.
- Inline rename in the tree row. It needs a change to the fork's tree widget; an input box is the closest the extension API allows.
- Dropping *between* rows. The tree reports only the row dropped on, so a drop on a page means "after it".

## Open decisions

- Whether `products` becomes a table later (versions open side by side).
- Whether the six sections stay fixed, or are defined per project in `manifest.json`.


---

---
title: "Shell and pages, v2 — the full layout, drawn"
order: 407
edit: false
---

This supersedes the shell, activity-bar and left-panel parts of [application-design.md](application-design). Every
surface below is drawn in full; nothing is summarised as "keeps the same frame".

Counted from the manifests before designing anything:

| | today | v2 |
|---|---|---|
| activity-bar containers | 5 | **4** |
| side-bar views | 19 | **7** |
| center pages | ~44 | **17** |
| commands | 250+ | 250+ (every id unchanged) |

Legend: `[ Button ]` is a button · `▸ ▾ •` are tree disclosure and items · `⋯` is a menu · `⬚` is a picture · `‹ ›` are
tabs · `◀` marks the selected row · `(•)` marks a radio choice.

---

## The four rules

1. **A side bar is a list you work in** — not a dashboard, not a status board. Status belongs to the status bar; actions
   belong to the view's title bar.
2. **A row is a thing, or it is an action — never both.** `test11.md` is a thing. *Build Center* is an action wearing a tree
   row at the same weight, which is why the column reads as noise.
3. **One name per concept.** Not *Needs You* here and *Review Center* there. Not *Publish* for both "copy a build" and "push
   to GitHub".
4. **A module with nothing to show gets no icon.** An empty container is a broken promise.

---

## 1. The window

```
┌────┬──────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┬────────────────────┐
│ ⌂  │ [view title]            ⋯  ＋ │                             │ AI                 │
│ ▤  │ [ one-line header / search ]  │      the page you are       │ ─────────────────  │
│ ⌗  │ ────────────────────────────  │      working in             │ you: …             │
│ ≡  │                              │                             │ ai: …              │
│    │     the list you work in     │  ┌───────────────────────┐  ├────────────────────┤
│    │                              │  │                       │  │ PAGE               │
│    │                              │  │                       │  │ Outline·Draft·Props│
├────┴──────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────┴────────────────────┤
│ PROBLEMS · OUTPUT · TERMINAL                                                          │
├───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ▤ Test1 · 148 pages · 1 owed · v1.4 · Build ✓ 10:42 · Pages ✓                          │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Left** one container, one list · **Center** one page · **Right** two secondary side bars that follow the page (§5) ·
**Bottom** the window's facts, once.

---

## 2. Activity bar: four icons

The activity bar is the whole application's top-level navigation: **one icon is one container, and a container is one side
bar.** That is the only structure VS Code gives an extension, so the bar is where "how many places does this application
have?" is decided.

```
 ┌────┬──────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │    │ GLYPH        │ TOOLTIP — what the icon is                               │
 ├────┼──────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │  1 │ house        │ Home — projects, recent activity, repositories, the site   │
 │  2 │ book         │ Docs — the documentation workspace                        │
 │  3 │ grid         │ Tools — SQL, BACnet, IO, network                          │
 │  4 │ sliders      │ Settings — settings and help                              │
 ├────┼──────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
 │  5 │ account      │ accounts                     (VS Code's own, not ours)    │
 │  6 │ gear         │ Manage                       (VS Code's own, not ours)    │
 └────┴──────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Rows 1–4 are ours; 5–6 belong to VS Code and cannot be removed. The whole bar can be collapsed by the user at any time, so
nothing here may be the *only* way to reach a page — every icon's contents are also in the command palette.

### 2.1 What each icon opens

| # | icon | tooltip | side bar it opens | the job it does | you reach for it when |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | house | **Home** | `Projects` · `Repositories` · `Recent activity` | orient: which project, which Temco code the docs are written from, what happened, what was published | you start a session, or you are not sure what to do next |
| 2 | book | **Docs** | `Pages` | write: the tree of pages, the two acts that ship them | you are working on the documentation, which is most of the time |
| 3 | grid | **Tools** | `Query & Browse` · `Schema & Maintenance` | diagnose: devices, IO points, BACnet objects, network, SQL | a controller is misbehaving, or you need data out of the system |
| 4 | sliders | **Settings** | `Settings` | configure: appearance, modules, models, publishing, help | something is wrong with the installation, or you are changing how it behaves |

The lists are unevenly split — **Home 3, Docs 1, Tools 2, Settings 1** — because that is the real number of lists that
hold things you click into (rule 1). An uneven bar is the honest bar: a container with one list is fine, a container kept
only so the bar looks symmetric is not.

### 2.2 Why these four, in this order

```
   orient ──▶ write ──▶ diagnose ──▶ configure
     ⌂          ▤           ⌗            ≡
   (daily)   (all day)    (sometimes)  (rarely)
```

- **Order follows a session, not a feature list.** Left to right is the order a person actually moves: find the project, do
  the work, investigate a problem, change a setting. Icons are muscle memory; the position is the documentation.
- **One icon per kind of activity, not per module.** Nine modules, four activities: that is why the count is four and not
  nine, and why adding a module does not add an icon.
- **The test for membership.** An icon earns its place only if it opens a list you *work in* and revisit regularly. If it
  opens one page you visit monthly, it is a row somewhere, not an icon. That test is what removed *Learn* (read-only output →
  a page under Home) and *AI* (see §2.4).

### 2.3 The Settings glyph: sliders, not a gear

```
   ours                          VS Code's
 ┌──────────────┐              ┌──────────────┐
 │   ══╪══      │  sliders     │      ⚙       │  Manage
 │   ══╪══      │              │              │
 └──────────────┘              └──────────────┘
      row 4                        row 6
```

A gear at 16 px is a grey blob; two gears in one column is one meaning too many, and the one at the bottom is not ours. So
the Settings container is drawn as **sliders** — the second universal conventions for preferences — and its tooltip reads
**Settings** where VS Code's reads *Manage*.

The container id stays `temcoSettings`, so saved layout and `onView` activation are untouched. The whole change is one file:
`media/settings.svg`.

### 2.4 What is deliberately *not* in the bar

| not here | where it is | why |
|---|---|---|
| **AI** | the right-hand secondary side bar (§4.16) | a conversation is not a destination; it is a thing you have *while* writing. Putting it on the left would make it a place you leave the page to visit |
| **Published site / Learn** | Home ▸ Published site (§4.6) | read-only output of work done elsewhere — a page, not a place to keep open |
| **Repositories** | the **Home** side bar (§4.4) | it is the shelf of **Temco code** that pages are written from — a list of things, opened while orienting, not an activity of its own. The drafting that reads it happens in Docs (§4.8a), and every draft names the file it was given |
| **BACnet · network · device manager** | inside Tools | they are jobs inside the diagnostic activity, not activities of their own — and two of them have no manifest yet, so they must not get an icon that opens nothing (rule 4) |

### 2.5 Before and after

Today the manifest declares five activity-bar containers, all from `temco-workbench`:

```
        TODAY (manifest)                        V2
┌────┐  ⌂ Home        (5 views) ───────▶  ┌────┐  ⌂ Home      (3 views)
│ ⌂  │  ▤ Docs        (6 views) ───────▶  │ ⌂  │  ▤ Docs      (1 view)
│ ▤  │  ✦ AI          (2 views) ───────▶  │ ▤  │  ⌗ Tools     (2 views)
│ ✦  │  ⌗ Tools       (2 views) ───────▶  │ ⌗  │  ≡ Settings  (1 view)
│ ⌗  │  ≡ Settings    (4 views)           │ ≡  │
│ ≡  │                                   └────┘
└────┘
  5 icons · 19 views                     4 icons · 7 views
```

Two things move — nothing disappears:

- **AI leaves the bar** for the right-hand side bar: the *assistant* and *runtime* views regroup there (§4.16), next to the
  draft panel that is already on that side.
- **Repositories changes container.** It is a view inside **Docs** today (the sixth one), which is why it never had an icon.
  It moves to **Home**, where "what can I read?" is one of the questions Home answers. Home's five sections today are mostly
  status boards; under this design it has three real lists — *Projects*, *Repositories*, *Recent activity* — while Docs drops
  to the single *Pages* tree (§4.4).

Home, Docs, Tools and Settings all stay. Their *contents* change (§3–§7), not their identity.

Two other containers exist today but were never in the bar, and this design keeps them out:

| container | location | what it is |
|---|---|---|
| `temcoAi` | secondary side bar (right) | the AI side bar — already where §2.4 wants AI |
| `temcoDraft` | auxiliary bar (right, bottom) | the AI draft panel, titled *Page* — it renders the page being written beside the page list |

**What the eight icons you saw were.** The earlier build also carried `temcoLearn`, `temcoHelp` and
`temco-repositories` containers. Those three were *removed* in the manifest fix that preceded this document, which is why
the bar reads five today: Learn → Home ▸ Published site, Help → Settings ▸ Help, Repositories → Home ▸ Repositories. So
the sequence is **8 → 5 (done, manifest) → 4 (this document, AI leaves)**.

### 2.6 States

```
   selected              hover                 no project yet
 ┌────┐               ┌────┐                ┌────┐
 │ ▤  │ ◀ accent bar  │ ▤  │ ◀ tint         │ ⌂  │ ◀ Home is where the
 │    │               │    │                │    │   answer is
 └────┘               └────┘                └────┘
```

- **Selected** — the icon for the open container carries VS Code's accent bar; clicking it again collapses the side bar.
- **No badge on any icon.** Counts belong to the status bar and to the view that owns them; a number on an icon is read as
  "notifications" and gets ignored after a week.
- **No empty container, ever** (rule 4): if a module has nothing to show it is not in the bar at all.

### 2.7 What implementing this touches

| change | risk |
|---|---|
| 5 containers → 4 (remove the AI container) | one manifest, no code — `onView` for its views moves with them |
| 19 views → 7 | views are removed only after their content exists elsewhere (§8); *Repositories* is **moved**, not removed |
| `media/settings.svg` redrawn as sliders | one icon file |
| container ids unchanged | saved layout, `onView` activation and every `when: view == …` clause keep working |
| `check-temco-modules` already refuses a view inside a container that does not exist | a half-finished step fails the build instead of showing an empty panel |

## 4. Every page, drawn in full

All pages share one frame, so a page is recognisable at a glance:

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ EYEBROW › HERE                                                               │
│ Title                                     [ secondary ]  [ primary action ]  │
│ one line saying why a person is on this page                                 │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  content                                                                     │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### The Home side bar — three lists under one icon

Home's six pages (§4.1–§4.6) are opened from this column; each section is a real view, so it collapses, filters and keeps
its own `⋯`:

```
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ Test1                        │  ← the project everything else is about
│ 148 pages · 2 sources        │
│ [ Find a project…          ] │
├──────────────────────────────┤
│ PROJECTS                   ⋯ │
│  ▸ Test1                ◀    │
├──────────────────────────────┤
│ REPOSITORIES               ⋯ │
│  ▸ temco-docs      main  ✓   │
│  ▸ temco-platform  main      │
│  ▸ docs/help       folder    │
├──────────────────────────────┤
│ RECENT ACTIVITY            ⋯ │
│  ⬆ Test1 v1.0       2 h      │
│  ✎ Commissioning    12 min   │
└──────────────────────────────┘
```

`Repositories` is the view today filed under **Docs**; it belongs here because "what can I read?" is a Home question.

### 4.1 Home ─ Projects

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ DASHBOARD › PROJECTS                                                         │
│ Projects                                          [ + New Project ]          │
│ Everything this machine documents. A project is what a release is named after │
│ and what every other page asks about.                                        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤

│  │ current            [▸] │  │ it here                │                      │

└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

│                      │                                                       │

│ ▸ From a repository  │   Version  [ 1.0.0                    ]               │
│   cite what we have  │                                                       │

│                      │            User guide - an Overview area, two topics  │
│                      │            and the registry                           │

```

### 4.3 Home ─ Recent activity

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ DASHBOARD › RECENT ACTIVITY                                                  │
│ Recent activity                                                              │
│ What changed lately, newest first. Read-only — no row acts.                   │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 10:42  ▶  built the site        Test1 · 148 pages → dist/site                │
│ 10:31  ✎  test11.md             Test1 › Overview                             │
│ 10:18  ⬆  published 1.0.0       Test1 · 8 files · ~/.temco/published/test1    │
│ 09:55  ⌁  read temco-docs       main · 148 pages · 2 errors                  │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### 4.4 Home ─ Repositories *(the Temco code the documentation is written from)*

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ HOME › REPOSITORIES                                                          │
│ Repositories                                      [ + Add a Repository… ]    │
│ The Temco repositories these pages are written from — one list, two acts:    │
│ read them to cite what they hold, or tick files and draft from the code.     │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ▽ temcocontrols                                          organisation        │
│  ▸ temco-platform   C:\QN\temcocontrols\temco-platform                       │
│     main · read 12 min ago · 148 pages · 2 errors  [Read] [Draft] [⋯]        │
│  ▸ temco-docs       C:\QN\temcocontrols\temco-docs                           │
│     main · never read · local copy on disk         [Read] [Draft] [⋯]        │
│  ▸ docs/help        D:\Contoso\docs\help                                     │
│     a folder · read yesterday · 64 pages           [Read] [Draft] [⋯]        │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ⋯ on a row: Set the branch · Open the local copy · Copy the URL ·            │
│             Remove the local copy · Forget it                                │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

The word on the row is *repository*; inside a project the same thing is called a *source* (§4.7), because that is what
it is there — one of the places a project cites. One entity, two scopes, and the list is the scope where you act on it.

| button | what it does | what it costs |
|---|---|---|
| **[ Read ]** | finds `docs/*/registry.json` inside it — or converts a folder once — and records the pages it holds | one API call, or one folder walk; **nothing is ever written to the source** |
| **[ Draft ]** | opens the file ticker (§4.5) so code files can be ticked as material for a new page (§4.8a) | nothing until *Draft* is pressed; a page exists only when the draft is accepted |

`[ Draft ]` is the button that makes this page part of writing rather than a library: the code in these repositories is the
raw material of every page that is not already written, and the material a page was given is recorded on the page.

**Why both a view and a page.** The view in the Home side bar is the everyday list — arrow keys, filter, open one. This
page is the same sources drawn wide, for what a tree row cannot hold: the organisation they came from,
`[ + Add a Repository… ]`, in bulk. One law on both: a row is a thing, an action is in its buttons or `⋯` (rule 2).

### 4.5 Home ─ Repository detail *(the file ticker: what a page can be written from)*

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ REPOSITORY                                                                   │
│ temco-platform          [ Copy the Clone URL ] [ Open on GitHub ] [ Refresh ]│
│ What this repository holds that documentation can be written from.           │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ [ main ]  [ 148 pages ]  [ 2 errors ]  [ read 12 minutes ago ]               │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ [ Download ]  [ Pull ]  [ Remove the Local Copy ]                            │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ CODE TO DRAFT FROM                                 [ Filter by path… ]       │
│ 3 ticked · 41 kB of 12,000     [ Code only ]  [ Select All ]  [ Clear ]      │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ☑ src/commissioning/wiring.ts     12 kB   the order a technician follows     │
│ ☑ src/commissioning/torque.ts      9 kB   limits and units                   │
│ ☑ src/safety/interlocks.ts        20 kB   what stops a run                   │
│ ☐ src/ui/panel.tsx                61 kB   too large — would be skipped       │
│ ☐ docs/help/registry.json          —      already read as 148 pages          │
│ ☐ package.json                     —      generated, never material          │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ [ Draft a Page from These 3 Files ]    [ Create a Project from These Files ] │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

This is the step the user asked for, made explicit: **code in, page out.** The list is honest about what it will cost —
generated, vendored and build output are never offered, and a file over the size budget is shown greyed with the reason
rather than silently dropped. What is ticked here is exactly what the draft is given (§4.8a), and the page that results
says so.

```

├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
```

```
│           A folder with a registry is cited as it stands; anything else is    │

└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### 4.8a Docs ─ New page *(material first — this is where code becomes prose)*

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│ DOCS › TEST1 › NEW PAGE                        [ Draft ]   [ ✕ ]             │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  Goes in      Overview/ ▾       File name   commissioning.md                 │
│  Title        Wiring the harness                                             │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  WHAT IS IT WRITTEN FROM?                                                    │
│   ( ) Nothing — a blank page                                                 │
│   (•) The pages already here    ids, titles and headings · 148 pages         │
│   ( ) A repository's code                                                    │
│        temco-platform ▾   [ main ]   [ Tick the files… ]  3 files · 41 kB    │
│        ☑ wiring.ts   ☑ torque.ts   ☑ interlocks.ts                           │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  WHAT SHOULD IT SAY?                                                         │
│  [ Explain how the harness is wired, in the order a technician follows. ]    │
├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│  12,000 characters of room · 3 files in · 1 file too large, left out         │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

Three things are decided on this page and nowhere else:

- **What the draft may read.** Three choices, and code is only one of them. The budget is stated, not discovered: 12,000
  characters, largest files first, and everything left out is named. Generated, vendored and build output are never offered.
- **What the draft is for.** One sentence in the person's words — the note, not a template.
- **What it is.** Where it goes, the file name and the title, so the page lands in the right place rather than being moved
  afterwards.

`[ Draft ]` writes nothing. It opens the proposal in the PAGE side bar (§4.18); the page exists when **Accept** is pressed,
and from that moment it carries one line of provenance — `temco-platform · src/commissioning/wiring.ts + 2 · 3f2a1c` — shown
on Properties, on **Review**, and in **Registry**.

### 4.18 The PAGE side bar (right): Outline · Draft · Properties

```
┌─────────────────────────────┐   ┌─────────────────────────────┐
│ PAGE                        │   │ DRAFT                       │
│ Outline │ Draft │ Properties│   │ draft │ preview │ properties│
├─────────────────────────────┤   ├─────────────────────────────┤
│ ▸ Overview                  │   │ The wiring table needs a     │
│ ▸ Commissioning             │   │ note about torque.           │
│   • Wiring the harness   ◀  │   │                              │
│   • First power-on          │   │ WRITTEN FROM                │
│ ▸ Reference                 │   │  temco-platform             │
│                             │   │  wiring.ts · torque.ts      │
│                             │   │  + 1 more · 41 kB · 3f2a1c  │
│                             │   │  [ Change the files… ]      │
│                             │   │                              │
│                             │   │ [ Accept ]   [ Discard ]     │
│                             │   │ [ Record in the Review Center]│
└─────────────────────────────┘   └─────────────────────────────┘
```

`WRITTEN FROM` is the answer to *"where did this text come from?"* — one line, every time: which repository, which files,
which commit, and how much of the budget they took. It is on the draft, on the finished page's `Properties`, on **Review**,
and on every row of **Registry**, so nobody has to trust a memory. `[ Change the files… ]` reopens the ticker (§4.5) without
asking the question again.

`Properties` is the page's registry values — title, owner, audience, ship, and that same `written from` line — edited in place.

## 5. How code, AI, the page and the draft fit together

Four things, four jobs. Only one of them is a place you navigate to; the rest happen where you already are:

```
  Repositories (§4.4, §4.5)      the shelf: Temco code, read or ticked
        │  tick files
        ▼
 ┌──────────────────┐   ┌──────────────────┐   ┌──────────────────┐
 │ MATERIAL §4.8a   │   │ DRAFT §4.18      │   │ PAGE §4.9        │
 │ 41 kB of 12,000  │──▶│ + WRITTEN FROM   │──▶│ *.md in Pages    │
 └──────────────────┘   └──────────────────┘   └──────────────────┘
                                              AI proposes; Accept writes
```

```
      right side bar                    center                      right side bar
┌───────────────────────────┐     ┌────────────────────┐     ┌───────────────────────────┐
│ AI                        │     │ the page           │     │ PAGE                      │
│ ask about the page,       │ ──▶ │ being written      │ ◀── │ Outline · Draft · Props   │
│ read its citations        │     │                    │     │ accept or discard a draft │
└───────────────────────────┘     └────────────────────┘     └───────────────────────────┘
   "help me understand"              "the work"                 "the proposed change"
```

A draft arrives in the PAGE side bar, is settled there, and also appears on **Review** while it waits. Nothing is written
until **Accept**; no draft is applied without a person. A draft that came from code never loses the thread back to it —
**Repo.** is one click from the draft, and one page of the same code file is one click from the repository (§4.5).

---

## 6. Where every action lives

| you want to… | you go to | not to |
|---|---|---|
| start a project | Home ▸ Projects ▸ **+ New Project** | a second "Projects Overview" page |
| read code from a repository | Home ▸ Repositories ▸ **[ Read ]** | the raw registry.json |
| write a page by hand | Docs ▸ Pages ▸ double-click, or `⋯` ▸ **New Page…** ▸ *Nothing* | a section called *Needs You* |
| write a page from code | Docs ▸ `⋯` ▸ **New Page…** ▸ *A repository's code* ▸ tick files | a separate *Drafting* page or container |
| settle a draft | the `1 owed` link, Ship ▸ Review, or the PAGE side bar | three different lists |
| build / release / deploy / export | `[Build]` `[⇪]` in the Docs header, or Ship | four separate pages |
| see why a page is not in the site | Docs ▸ `⋯` ▸ **Registry** | the raw registry.json |
| read what was published | Home ▸ Published site | a *Site* view |
| change a setting | Settings ▸ Settings (left rail) | a container with four views |
| find a shortcut or the logs | Settings ▸ Help | a *Help* container with two views |
| ask about a page | the AI side bar (right) | an activity-bar icon |

Every command id is unchanged, so the palette still reaches all 250.

---

## 7. What is removed, and what absorbs it

| removed | absorbed by |
|---|---|
| *Project* side-bar section | the one-line header in Docs |
| *Needs You* view + Needs You page | **Review** page + the `1 owed` link |
| *Sources* view | the **Project** page (already lists sources with branches) |
| *Publish* view (3 action rows) | `[Build]` `[⇪]` in the header, and `⋯ ▸ Build Center / Releases / Exports` |
| *Repositories* view | **moves** from Docs to Home (still a view; the page stays for the wide work) |
| *Overview* view | **Home ▸ Recent activity** + the project cards |
| *Site* / *Delivery* views | **Home ▸ Published site** page |
| *Profile* / *Platform* / *Learn* / *Support* views | **Settings** page + **Help** page |
| **AI** activity-bar container | the AI secondary side bar (§4.16) |
| Build Center · Releases · Exports pages | **Ship** tabs (bodies moved, not rewritten) |
| 5 AI pages | **AI ▸ Settings** with four tabs (§4.17) |
| 4 registry commands | **Registry** page (the raw file stays one click away) |

19 side-bar views → **7**. ~44 pages → **17**. Nothing is deleted before its content exists elsewhere.

---

## 8. Order of work

1. **Docs panel** — 5 regions → header + tree + `⋯`. No page touched; reversible.
2. **Home** — three lists (Projects · Repositories · Recent) and their pages, including Published site (§4.3, §4.4, §4.6). The
   *Repositories* view changes container only; no code moves. On the page, `[ Draft ]` is added beside `[ Read ]`.
3. **Drafting loop** — the ticker on the repository page (§4.5), the material picker on *New Page* (§4.8a) and `WRITTEN FROM`
   on the draft (§4.18). The parts exist today (`newPagePage.ts`, `codeFiles`/`readMaterials`); this step names them in the UI
   and records the line on the accepted page.
4. **Settings + Help** — two pages with rails (§4.20, §4.21); four views retire.
5. **Ship** — one page, four tabs (§4.15); the three bodies move as they are.
6. **Quality** — Review and Registry (§4.12, §4.13); retire the *Needs You* view and four registry commands.
7. **AI** — the side bar and one settings page (§4.16, §4.17); the activity-bar icon goes.

After each step `npm run check-temco` refuses a view inside a container that no longer exists, so a half-finished step fails
the build instead of showing an empty panel.

---

## 9. Decisions taken here

- **Published site lives in Home** (§4.6) — decided.
- **Four activity-bar icons, kept as designed** — Home · Docs · Tools · Settings. The Settings container keeps its own icon
  but is **drawn as sliders instead of a gear**, because VS Code's own *Manage* gear sits at the bottom of the same column;
  two gears is one meaning too many. Id unchanged, so saved layout survives.
- **Tools stays in the activity bar** with both views untouched, because 94 commands and two of its modules have no manifest
  yet; hiding it would make existing work unreachable.
- **Settings and Help are pages, not containers** (§4.20, §4.21) — decided.
- **Repositories stays a view, under Home** (§2.5, §4.4) — it is a list you work in, so it keeps the free filter, arrow keys
  and collapse of a native view; its page adds the two per-row acts. **It is the shelf of Temco code the documentation is
  written from**, not a library of finished docs: `[ Read ]` cites what a repository already holds, `[ Draft ]` turns ticked
  code files into material for a new page (§4.5 → §4.8a → §4.18).
- **Drafting always starts on one page** — *New Page* (§4.8a) — where the material is chosen before a word is proposed. The
  budget (12,000 characters) and everything left out are stated on that page, and the accepted page keeps a one-line
  `written from` record (repository · files · commit) wherever it is shown. Provenance is part of the page, not a note about it.
- **Ship replaces Build Center, Releases and Exports in one move** (§4.15) — decided. The three bodies move into the four
  tabs unchanged; the three commands stay registered so anything bound to them keeps working, and each opens Ship on its
  own tab.

Nothing in this document has been implemented. Step 1 is the Docs panel, and it waits for a go-ahead.

---

## 10. Look and feel

> **Superseded by a proposal awaiting confirmation:** [welcome-v3-design.md](welcome-v3-design) — Fluent 2 tokens with
> Azure's purchase-options anatomy (three option cards, a brand-tinted wash, numbers, one callout, questions). Read that
> first; the section below describes the version now in the code, which v3 replaces.

The look is the one a developer reads every day (platform.openai.com/docs): **a big quiet title, one line of grey, pill
buttons, and content held in soft panels.** What it replaced was a coloured band with a gradient and a glow; it made the
first screen shout and left every page under it looking unfinished, which is exactly what was wrong with it.

**Implemented.** The rules are in `extensions/temco-ui/media/page.css` (the "document page" layer), the markup in
`extensions/temco-ui/src/components.ts`, and Welcome · Projects · Overview · Documents use it today.

```
  Documentation
  Write the pages of a product, draft them from the code that runs it,
  and publish a site a customer can open.

  [ Search ]  Find a page, a file or a command…            Ctrl P
  ( Create a project )   ( Read a source )

  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Start with a project               │ TypeScript                              │
  │ A project holds the pages, the     │  1  // a page, drafted from the code    │
  │ sources they are read from, and    │  2  const page = await temco.draft({    │
  │ the artifacts a build produces.    │  3    from: 'src/wiring.ts',            │
  │                                    │  4    into: 'guide/wiring.md',          │
  │ ( New project )   ( Add a source ) │  5  });                                 │
  │                                                                            │
  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Draft a page from the repository it documents    ( Read a Source )         │
  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Build paths                                        [ All Commands ]
  ┌───────────┐ ┌───────────┐ ┌───────────┐ ┌───────────┐
  │ ▣ Create… │ │ ▣ Read…   │ │ ▣ Open…   │ │ ▣ Review… │
  └───────────┘ └───────────┘ └───────────┘ └───────────┘

                                                     ( ✦ Ask AI )

  a 34px title, a 15px grey line, pill buttons, a soft panel, a code card,
  a banner, and four dense tiles
```

### 10.1 Near-monochrome, and why

| surface | colour | why |
|---|---|---|
| the page | the theme's own background | a page is not a poster |
| the panel and the banner | `--bg-inset` / `--bg-card` | one step in from the page, no fill colours |
| borders | `--border` | a hairline, never a shadow |
| the one filled button | **the theme's foreground** — black pill in a light theme, white in a dark one | it is the thing to press, and it survives both themes without a second rule |
| the accent (`--az-blue` `#0078d4`) | a link, the 3px rule on the leading figure, a focus ring | three places on a page, or it stops meaning anything |
| syntax in the code card | four tones mixed against the page | reads in a dark theme, and is the only saturated colour on the screen |

**No gradient, no glow, no brand band.** The check fails the build if a gradient reappears in this layer.

### 10.2 Type does the work

| role | size / weight | note |
|---|---|---|
| page title | 34px / 600, `-0.028em` | 27px under 900px wide |
| panel heading | 20px / 600 | the `h2` a section is told apart by |
| body | 15px / 1.7 for the page's line, `--fs-sm` for supporting text | still follows the workbench font |
| code | 12.5px / 1.75, tabular line numbers | numbers drawn, never written into the text |
| small caps, letter-spacing, chips | **none** | the one thing the previous version over-used |

### 10.3 The eight components, and the gate that holds them to the stylesheet

`hero()` (the title block: eyebrow · `h1` · line · field · buttons · figures) · `panel()` (soft box + the code card
beside it) · `code()` (numbered, four tones) · `banner()` (one sentence + one action) · `kpi()` · `section()` ·
`cards(items, { dense })` · `floatAction()`. All are published by `temco-ui` and reached through each module's `ui.ts`,
the same way `page()` and `icon()` are.

`scripts/check-components.mts` runs in `npm run compile` (`check-temco`) and fails the build when:

- a class the components emit has **no rule** in `page.css`;
- a value a person typed reaches the document **unescaped**;
- an option left out still renders an empty element;
- **the code card edits the sample** — the gate parses the rendered card back into plain text and compares it with the
  source lines, because line numbers and colour are drawn, not written;
- a surface stops taking its background from the theme, or the filled button stops being the theme's foreground;
- a **gradient** appears in the page layer.

### 10.4 Where it is used now

| page | title block | panel | banner | figures | sections |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Welcome (`temcoWorkbench.help.welcome`) | yes — with the field and two buttons | yes — "Start with a project" + the drafting sample | yes — the draft-from-repository line | — | *Build paths* (4 tiles) · *Pick up where you left off* · *Guides* (3 walks) · floating **Ask AI** |
| Projects (`temcoDocs.projects`) | yes — with two buttons and four figures | — | — | in the title block | *All Projects* |
| Overview (`temcoWorkbench.platformView`) | yes — two buttons, four figures | — | — | in the title block | *Documentation Pipeline*, feeds, cards |
| Documents (`temcoDocs.documents`) | — (page head kept) | — | — | yes — pages · read · missing · problems | — |

Next surfaces to take it: the AI dashboard, the Tools pages, the Ship tabs.

### 10.5 Translating pages, and translating the interface

Two different jobs, kept apart on purpose.

**Pages** — `Temco Docs: Translate This Page…` turns the page in front into a sibling file (`setup.md` → `setup.de.md`)
using an Azure AI Translator resource. The service is given *sentences*, never the file: `translate.ts` cuts the page into
segments whose concatenation reproduces it exactly, sends only the prose, and puts everything back — code fences, inline
code, table rules, link targets, image paths, front matter, line markers. That invariant is a build gate, not a comment.
`scripts/check-translate.mts` proves it with a fake transport that echoes its input: the page comes back byte for byte, a
fenced sample never appears in what was sent, and each failure a person can meet (401, 403, 429, unreachable, a short
answer) is a sentence about what to do. Proved by negative test: removing the front-matter rule fails the gate by name.

The key is a secret in the OS keychain (`context.secrets`); the endpoint and region are settings — where the resource
lives is configuration, who may use it is not.

**Interface** — strings on the new surfaces go through `vscode.l10n.t()`, so a bundle can be dropped in without touching
code. The rest of the application is still plain English; the rule from here is that a string a person reads is marked.


---

---
title: "The front page — the Azure portal's palette and scale"
order: 408
edit: false
---

**Status: implemented, corrected after review.** The page is the **platform description** — titled `Temco Platform`, with no breadcrumb — in the **Azure portal's own palette and type scale** (24px page title, 18px section, 14px card, 13px text; `#0078d4` blue, `#1f2327` ink, `#faf9f8` page, `#edebe9` hairline), not the marketing scale of §2. The rules are in `extensions/temco-ui/media/page.css`; the markup in `extensions/temco-ui/src/components.ts`.

---

## 0. What went wrong twice, so it does not go wrong a third time

| attempt | what it was | why it was wrong |
|---|---|---|
| v1 | a navy→blue gradient band with a cyan glow and a grid | that is a marketing *poster*. Nothing on the Azure pricing page looks like it, and it made the first screen shout |
| v2 | the OpenAI documentation page from the screenshot | calm and readable, but it turned the front page into a *reading* page and dropped the one thing the Azure page is built on: **three options side by side, each with ticks and a button** |

**v3** is the Azure account / purchase-options page read as five bands, built out of Microsoft's own Fluent 2 numbers (taken
from the Fluent 2 design-system stylesheet, not invented): a tinted wash behind the title, three option cards, a numbers
strip, one tinted callout, and questions at the bottom.

---

## 1. The reference, as five bands

```
  ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Home › Pricing › Purchase options                          ← breadcrumb 12px │
  │                                                                              │
  │  Choose how you want to pay for Azure                           ← H1 40/52    │
  │  Pay only for what you use, or commit and save. Every option starts with      │
  │  the same free grant.                                                         │
  │                                                                              │
  │ [ Search pricing… ]                      ( Create an account )  ← 40px, 4px  │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │  ┌──────────────────┐ ┌──────────────────┐ ┌──────────────────┐               │
  │  │ ▣ Pay as you go  │ │ ▣ Savings plan   │ │ ▣ Reservations   │  ← 3 option  │
  │  │ one line of what │ │ ┃ recommended    │ │ one line         │    cards 20/28│
  │  │ ✓ tick one       │ │ ✓ tick one       │ │ ✓ tick one       │               │
  │  │ ✓ tick two       │ │ ✓ tick two       │ │ ✓ tick two       │  ← 14/20     │
  │  │ ✓ tick three     │ │ ✓ tick three     │ │ ✓ tick three     │               │
  │  │ ( Get started )  │ │ ( Get started )  │ │ ( Get started )  │  ← button 32px│
  │  └──────────────────┘ └──────────────────┘ └──────────────────┘               │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │   99.99%    │   60+     │    $200      │   1,000s     ← numbers 24/32           │
  │   uptime    │  regions  │  free grant  │   customers                              │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐      │
  │  │ ▣  One tinted callout: a sentence and a link            Learn more │           │
  │  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘      │
  ├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │  Questions                                      ← section 24/32 semibold      │
  │  ▸ What counts as a free service?                                            │
  │  ▸ Can I change options later?                                               │
  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

What makes it *that* page, in order of importance:

1. **The wash.** A soft brand tint behind the title, fading into the page — not a saturated band, not a photo.
2. **Three cards in a row, the same height, each ending in a button.** The middle one is marked, not coloured in.
3. **Ticks, not paragraphs.** Three short lines per card.
4. **Numbers in a row** under the cards.
5. **One tinted callout** in the whole page.
6. **Questions at the bottom**, in rows, not in a dialog.

---

## 2. The tokens — Microsoft's own numbers

Taken from the Fluent 2 design-system stylesheet (`--fontSizeHero800`, `--colorNeutralBackground1`, …), so the page is
the system rather than an imitation of it.

### Type

| role | token | size / line | weight |
|---|---|---|---|
| page title (H1) | Hero900 | **40 / 52** | 600 |
| panel & section title (H2) | Base600 | **24 / 32** | 600 |
| card title (H3) | Base500 | **20 / 28** | 600 |
| intro paragraph | Base400 | **16 / 22** | 400 |
| body & card copy | Base300 | **14 / 20** | 400 |
| caption, breadcrumb, label | Base200 | **12 / 16** | 400 |
| code | — | **12.5 / 20** | 400 mono |

Font family: the workbench font in the app (as now), with **Segoe UI** named first in the fallback chain.

### Space, radius, elevation

| | values |
|---|---|
| spacing | 2 · 4 · 6 · 8 · 10 · 12 · 16 · 20 · 24 · 32 · 40 · 48 |
| radius | 2 · 4 (controls) · 8 (cards) · 12 (the one big panel) · pill |
| shadow | `2`: `0 0 2px rgba(0,0,0,.12), 0 1px 2px rgba(0,0,0,.14)` · `4` · `8` · `16` — the reference uses **4** on a resting card and **8** on hover |

### Colour — light

| role | value | used for |
|---|---|---|
| brand | **`#0f6cbd`** | the primary button, links, the recommended card's stroke |
| brand hover / pressed | `#115ea3` / `#0c3b5e` | states |
| brand tint | **`#ebf3fc`** | the wash behind the title, the callout, the recommended card's fill |
| text 1 / 2 / 3 | `#242424` / `#616161` / `#707070` | headings / body / captions |
| surface page / card / subtle | `#fafafa` / `#ffffff` / `#f5f5f5` | page / cards / inset bars |
| stroke | `#d1d1d1` | hairlines; `#616161` when it must be seen |
| success / warning / danger | `#0e700e` / `#bc4b09` / `#d13438` | state only, never decoration |

### Colour — dark (Windows dark)

| role | value |
|---|---|
| brand / hover / tint | `#115ea3` / `#479ef5` / `#082338` |
| text 1 / 2 / 3 | `#ffffff` / `#d6d6d6` / `#adadad` |
| surface page / card / subtle | `#1f1f1f` / `#292929` / `#333333` |
| stroke | `#666666` |

### What the theme owns, and what Azure owns

| | owner | why |
|---|---|---|
| page background, card background, strokes, text | **the VS Code theme** (`--vscode-*`), with the Fluent value as the fallback | a person's editor theme must not be argued with |
| the brand blue, its tint, the type ramp, spacing, radius, shadows, the wash | **Fluent 2 / Azure**, fixed | this is the identity; it is the same in both themes |
| the wash | brand tint → transparent, 160px tall, behind the title only | it is the one thing that makes the top of the page read as Azure |

---

Notes on the drawing:

- **The wash** sits behind the title band only, 160px tall, `#ebf3fc → transparent`, and it is the *only* decoration on
  the page.
- **The three cards are the page.** They are the Azure purchase-option cards with our three acts.
- The **middle card is recommended** — filled with the tint and stroked with brand, because drafting from code is the
  thing this product does that others do not.
- **No panel with a code sample** on the front page: the code moved to where it belongs (the Drafting page, `§4.8a`), and
  the front page stopped competing with it. This is the change I most want confirmed.

---
